===== .changeset/r20-role-compiler.md =====
---
"@olympus-ai/compiler": minor
"@olympus-ai/core": minor
"@olympus-ai/vault": minor
"@olympus-ai/api": minor
"@olympus-ai/driver-claude-code": minor

---

R20: role compiler. Every seat runs under instructions a person wrote, compiled
from a roles directory at admission and pinned by hash (D-A-RC-01 to D-A-RC-06).

- New package `@olympus-ai/compiler`, depending on core alone:
  `compileRoles(directory)` reads one `<role-id>.md` per role and returns each
  as a `CompiledRole` with the SHA-256 of its bytes, or every entry it refused
  (`unreadable`, `not-regular`, `invalid-name`, `too-large` past 32 KiB,
  `not-utf8`, `empty`), each named. It ships `builder` and `reviewer` as
  `DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY`.
- core: `CompiledRole` gains `sha256`. `TaskRequest.role` is a `CompiledRole`,
  and `spawnSubagent` takes one. `DRIVER_CONTRACT_VERSION` is 2.0.0.
- vault: `AdmissionRecord.roles` pins every compiled role, and both Vaults
  refuse a record without it. `UsageRecord.role` names the role id and hash
  each call ran under (`RoleVersion`). `AdmissionRefusal` gains
  `roles-refused` and `role-undefined`.
- api: `RunRequest.roles` is the roles directory's absolute path, compiled at
  admission. A directory inside the workspace or the workspace store is
  refused (`overlaps`); a broken entry refuses as `roles-refused`, and a graph
  naming a role with no file, or a run with no `reviewer`, as `role-undefined`,
  before any driver call. The review seat runs under `reviewer` with its
  task's grants. A resume refuses a record with no roles. The server takes
  `rolesDirectory`, and the host reads `FACTORY_ROLES_DIR`.
- driver-claude-code: every task's role is rendered on the argv as the agent
  the session runs as (`--agents`, `--agent`), with its instructions and no
  other field; a session that did not load it is refused.

diff --git a/packages/api/src/http/server.ts b/packages/api/src/http/server.ts

 export interface ApiServerOptions {
   readonly components: BuiltGraph;
   /** The local token every request must bear. At least 32 characters; a shorter one is refused at construction. */
   readonly token: string;

 export interface ApiServer {
@@ -160,247 +162,247 @@ function refusalStatus(outcome: Exclude<RunOutcome, { ok: true }>): number {
 export function createApiServer(options: ApiServerOptions): ApiServer {
   if (typeof options.token !== 'string' || options.token.length < MIN_TOKEN_LENGTH) {
     throw new Error(`api server: the local token must be at least ${String(MIN_TOKEN_LENGTH)} characters`);
   }
   if (typeof options.principal !== 'string' || options.principal.trim() === '') throw new Error('api server: principal must name who the token is');
   const expected = digest(`Bearer ${options.token}`);
   const { components, principal } = options;
   const runs = new Map<RunId, Tracked>();
   const open = new Set<ServerResponse>();
 
   const tracked = (runId: RunId): Tracked => {
     let entry = runs.get(runId);
     if (entry === undefined) {
       entry = { drive: null, cancel: null, lastOutcome: null, streams: new Set() };
       runs.set(runId, entry);
     }
     return entry;
   };
 
   const publish = (entry: Tracked, event: RunEvent): void => {
     for (const stream of entry.streams) stream(event);
   };
 
   /** Drives a run in the background; the returned promise never rejects. */
   const drive = (runId: RunId, start: (hooks: DriveHooks) => Promise<RunOutcome>): void => {
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     entry.cancel = null;
     const hooks: DriveHooks = {
       cancelRequested: () => entry.cancel,
       onCommit: (state) => { publish(entry, { event: 'state', data: state }); },
     };
     entry.drive = start(hooks)
       .then((outcome): DriveEnd => ({ kind: 'outcome', outcome }), (error: unknown): DriveEnd => ({ kind: 'error', message: describe(error) }))
       .then((end) => (end.kind === 'error' ? halt(runId, end) : end))
       .then(async (end) => {
         entry.lastOutcome = end;
         entry.drive = null;
         entry.cancel = null;
         const standing = await runStanding(components.vault, runId).then((s) => s.standing, () => undefined);
         if (standing !== undefined) publish(entry, { event: 'standing', data: standing });
         publish(entry, { event: 'end', data: end });
       });
   };
 
   /**
    * A drive that ended in an error, rather than a refusal the line committed,
    * left the run's state as it was mid-step, which status would read as open
    * and a cancel would relabel. The halt is committed so the stop is the
    * Vault's record, and survives this process (A-P9-02). A run already
    * cancelled keeps that record instead; if the halt cannot be committed, the
    * drive's end says so.
    */
   const halt = async (runId: RunId, end: DriveEnd & { kind: 'error' }): Promise<DriveEnd> => {
     try {
       const state = await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
       if (state.cancelled !== null || state.halted !== null) return end;
       await components.vault.commitRunState({ ...state, halted: { at: new Date().toISOString(), message: end.message } }, state.version);
       return end;
     } catch (error) {
       return { kind: 'error', message: `${end.message}; the halt could not be recorded: ${describe(error)}` };
     }
   };
 
   /** The per-run report, read from the Vault alone (D-I1b-07). */
   const report = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunReport> => {
     try {
       await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
     } catch (error) {
       throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no run ${runId}: ${describe(error)}`);
     }
     return runReport(components.vault, runId);
   };
 
   const view = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunView> => {
     // Taken before the read, not after: a drive that ends while the state is read has committed
     // past what was read, and a view saying `driving: false` beside that state would present a
     // stale standing as settled. Read first, a drive running at the start is still reported.
     const entry = runs.get(runId);
     const driving = entry?.drive != null;
     const lastOutcome = entry?.lastOutcome ?? null;
     let read: { state: RunState; standing: RunView['standing'] };
     try {
       read = await runStanding(components.vault, runId);
     } catch (error) {
       throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no run ${runId}: ${describe(error)}`);
     }
     return { runId, state: read.state, standing: read.standing, driving, lastOutcome };
   };
 
   const create = async (body: CreateRunBody): Promise<CreatedRun> => {
     const policy = await loadPolicyFile(options.policyFile);
     if (!policy.ok) throw new HttpError(422, 'refused', `the server's policy file is refused: ${policy.message}`, policy);
     const runId = randomUUID() as RunId;
-    const admission = await admitRun({ runId, ...body, policy: policy.policy, components });
+    const admission = await admitRun({ runId, ...body, policy: policy.policy, roles: options.rolesDirectory, components });
     if (!admission.ok) throw new HttpError(refusalStatus(admission), 'refused', `run refused: ${admission.reason}`, admission);
     const { admitted } = admission;
     drive(runId, (hooks) => admitted.drive(hooks));
     return { runId, worstCase: admitted.worstCase, state: admitted.state };
   };
 
   const approve = async (runId: RunId, body: ApproveBody): Promise<RunView> => {
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     if (entry.drive !== null) throw new HttpError(409, 'conflict', `run ${runId} is being driven; it is not waiting for an approval`);
     await view(runId);
     const outcome = await approveStation({ runId, key: body.key, approvedBy: principal, vault: components.vault });
     if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'invalid-request' ? 400 : 409, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
     drive(runId, (hooks) => resumeRun({ runId, components }, hooks));
     return view(runId);
   };
 
   const cancel = async (runId: RunId): Promise<RunView> => {
     await view(runId);
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     const inFlight = entry.drive;
     if (inFlight !== null) {
       entry.cancel = { by: principal, at: new Date().toISOString() };
       await inFlight;
       const after = await view(runId);
       if (after.state.cancelled === null) {
         throw new HttpError(409, 'refused', `run ${runId} finished before the cancellation was taken; there is nothing to cancel`, after.standing);
       }
       return after;
     }
     const outcome = await cancelRun({ runId, by: principal, vault: components.vault });
     if (!outcome.ok) throw new HttpError(outcome.reason === 'finished' ? 409 : 400, 'refused', outcome.message, outcome);
     const now = await view(runId);
     publish(entry, { event: 'state', data: now.state });
     publish(entry, { event: 'standing', data: now.standing });
     return now;
   };
 
   const events = async (runId: RunId, res: ServerResponse): Promise<void> => {
     const now = await view(runId);
     const entry = tracked(runId);
     res.writeHead(200, { 'content-type': 'text/event-stream; charset=utf-8', 'cache-control': 'no-store', connection: 'keep-alive' });
     open.add(res);
     const write = (event: RunEvent): void => {
       res.write(`event: ${event.event}\ndata: ${JSON.stringify(event.data)}\n\n`);
     };
     const finish = (): void => {
       entry.streams.delete(stream);
       open.delete(res);
       res.end();
     };
     const stream = (event: RunEvent): void => {
       write(event);
       if (event.event === 'end') finish();
     };
     write({ event: 'state', data: now.state });
     write({ event: 'standing', data: now.standing });
     if (entry.drive === null) {
       write({ event: 'end', data: entry.lastOutcome });
       open.delete(res);
       res.end();
       return;
     }
     entry.streams.add(stream);
     res.on('close', () => {
       entry.streams.delete(stream);
       open.delete(res);
     });
   };
 
   const route = async (req: IncomingMessage, res: ServerResponse): Promise<void> => {
     const presented = digest(req.headers.authorization ?? '');
     if (!timingSafeEqual(presented, expected)) throw new HttpError(401, 'unauthorized', 'a valid local token is required');
     const url = new URL(req.url ?? '/', 'http://server.invalid');
     const parts = url.pathname.split('/').filter((p) => p !== '');
     const method = req.method ?? 'GET';
     if (parts[0] === 'outcomes' && parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await outcomes(components.vault, outcomeWindow(url.searchParams)));
       return;
     }
     if (parts[0] !== 'runs' || parts.length > 3) throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no route ${url.pathname}`);
     if (parts.length === 1) {
       if (method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 201, await create(createBody(await readJson(req))));
       return;
     }
     const runId = decodeURIComponent(parts[1] ?? '') as RunId;
     const action = parts[2];
     if (action === undefined) {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await view(runId));
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'report') {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, await report(runId));
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'events') {
       if (method !== 'GET') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       await events(runId, res);
       return;
     }
     if (action === 'approve' || action === 'cancel') {
       if (method !== 'POST') throw new HttpError(405, 'method-not-allowed', `${method} ${url.pathname}`);
       send(res, 200, action === 'approve' ? await approve(runId, approveBody(await readJson(req))) : await cancel(runId));
       return;
     }
     throw new HttpError(404, 'not-found', `no route ${url.pathname}`);
   };
 
   const server: Server = createServer((req, res) => {
     route(req, res).catch((error: unknown) => {
       const http = error instanceof HttpError ? error : new HttpError(500, 'internal', describe(error));
       if (res.headersSent) {
         res.end();
         return;
       }
       sendError(res, http);
     });
   });
 
   return {
     listen: (port, host) => new Promise((resolveUrl, reject) => {
       server.once('error', reject);
       server.listen(port, host, () => {
         server.off('error', reject);
         const address = server.address();
         if (address === null || typeof address === 'string') {
           reject(new Error('api server: listening on something other than a TCP port'));
           return;
         }
         const shown = address.family === 'IPv6' ? `[${address.address}]` : address.address;
         resolveUrl(`http://${shown}:${String(address.port)}`);
       });
     }),
     close: async () => {
       for (const res of open) res.end();
       open.clear();
       await new Promise<void>((done) => {
         server.close(() => { done(); });
         server.closeAllConnections();
       });
       await Promise.all([...runs.values()].flatMap((entry) => (entry.drive === null ? [] : [entry.drive])));
     },
   };
 }
 
 /**
  * An ISO 8601 date-time with `Z` or an offset. Without a zone `Date.parse`
  * reads a time in the server's own, so one request would name a different
  * window on every host.
  */

@@ -124,28 +125,33 @@ import {
 export interface LineContext {
   readonly run: Run;
   readonly policy: Policy;
+  /** Every role as admission compiled and pinned it; the only source of a seat's instructions (D-A-RC-01). */
+  readonly roles: readonly CompiledRole[];
   readonly artifacts: AdmissionRecord['artifacts'];
   readonly graph: TaskGraph;
   readonly checks: readonly CheckSpec[];
   /** The spec's security part as admitted, or null where none was and `spec` refuses (R17). */
   readonly securityPart: SecurityPart | null;
   readonly components: BuiltGraph;
   /** The digest of the base the runtime snapshotted at admission. */
   readonly baseTreeSha256: string;
   /** Replaced by every commit. */
   state: RunState;
   /**
    * Asked between steps, never during one: a cancellation it returns is
    * committed by the line itself, so no step in flight loses a commit to a
    * version race, and the usage record of a call already made is kept (D-P9-04).
    */
   readonly cancelRequested?: () => RunCancellation | null;
   /** Told of every state the line commits, after the Vault stored it. Serves the event stream; it cannot change what is committed. */
   readonly onCommit?: (state: RunState) => void;

 async function build(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {

 async function review(ctx: LineContext, task: Task): Promise<StationRefusal | undefined> {

 /** The workspace store's root may not overlap the workspace it snapshots: a task would then be handed a tree that holds its own history. */
 function storeProblems(req: RunRequest): RequestProblem[] {
   const { root } = req.components.workspaces;
   if (!within(root, req.workspace) && !within(req.workspace, root)) return [];
   return [{ path: 'components.workspaces', code: 'overlaps', message: `the workspace store ${root} and the workspace ${req.workspace} overlap; each must lie outside the other` }];
 }
 
+/** `path` with every link resolved, or as given where it cannot be: a directory that does not exist is refused when it is compiled. */
+async function resolved(path: string): Promise<string> {
+  return realpath(path).catch(() => resolve(path));
+}
+
+/**
+ * The roles directory may lie inside neither the workspace nor the workspace
+ * store, nor hold either: a copy of what is there is what a sandbox mounts,
+ * and a role there is one an agent could rewrite. Compared with every link
+ * resolved, so a link is no way around it (I1, D-R20-04).
+ */
+async function rolesProblems(req: RunRequest): Promise<RequestProblem[]> {
+  if (typeof req.roles !== 'string' || req.roles === '' || !isAbsolute(req.roles)) {
+    return [{ path: 'roles', code: 'not-absolute', message: 'roles must be the absolute path of the roles directory' }];
+  }
+  const roles = await resolved(req.roles);
+  const places = [['workspace', req.workspace], ['workspace store', req.components.workspaces.root]] as const;
+  for (const [name, place] of places) {
+    const outer = await resolved(place);
+    if (within(outer, roles) || within(roles, outer)) {
+      return [{ path: 'roles', code: 'overlaps', message: `the roles directory ${req.roles} and the ${name} ${place} overlap; roles are read from the host, outside every tree a sandbox mounts` }];
+    }
+  }
+  return [];
+}
+
+/**
+ * The roles directory compiled, and every seat the graph schedules given a
+ * role, before any driver is invoked (D-A-RC-06). Every role the graph names
+ * is checked, and `reviewer` as well, since every review seat runs under it.
+ */
+async function admitRoles(
+  directory: string, graph: TaskGraph,
+): Promise<{ ok: true; roles: readonly CompiledRole[] } | Extract<AdmissionOutcome, { reason: 'roles-refused' | 'role-undefined' }>> {
+  const compiled = await compileRoles(directory);
+  if (!compiled.ok) return { ok: false, reason: 'roles-refused', refused: compiled.refused };
+  const defined = new Set<string>(compiled.roles.map((r) => r.role));
+  const needed = [...new Set([...graph.tasks.map((t) => t.role), REVIEWER_ROLE])].sort();
+  const missing = needed.filter((role) => !defined.has(role));
+  if (missing.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'role-undefined', roles: missing };
+  return { ok: true, roles: compiled.roles };
+}

 /** A refused admission as the enforcement record holds it: the typed fields a reader counts by, and no caller text. */
 function admissionRefusalOf(outcome: Exclude<AdmissionOutcome, { ok: true }>): AdmissionRefusal {
   switch (outcome.reason) {
     case 'invalid-request':
       return problemCodes(outcome.problems);
     case 'unsafe-above-l1':
       return { reason: 'unsafe-above-l1', requestedLevel: outcome.requestedLevel, components: outcome.unsafe.map((u) => u.component) };
     case 'cost-unapproved':
       return { reason: 'cost-unapproved', requestedLevel: outcome.requestedLevel, worstCaseUsd: outcome.worstCase.usd, approvedCostUsd: outcome.approvedCostUsd };
     case 'controls-unavailable':
       return { reason: 'controls-unavailable', requestedLevel: outcome.requestedLevel, unavailable: outcome.unavailable };
     case 'policy-refused':

+      // Each entry is a name in the host's roles directory, which a person wrote; the message is left out, as an invalid request's is.
+      return { reason: 'roles-refused', refused: outcome.refused.map(({ entry, code }) => ({ entry, code })) };
+    case 'role-undefined':
+      return { reason: 'role-undefined', roles: [...outcome.roles] };
     case 'refused':
       return { reason: 'refused', at: outcome.at, refusal: outcome.transition };

 /** Every check admission makes, in order; `admitRun` records whichever refuses. */
 async function admission(req: RunRequest): Promise<AdmissionOutcome> {
   const problems = [...requestProblems(req), ...storeProblems(req)];
   if (problems.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems };
 
   const unsafe = unsafeComponents(req.components);
   if (unsafe.length > 0 && req.requestedLevel > 1) {
     return { ok: false, reason: 'unsafe-above-l1', requestedLevel: req.requestedLevel, unsafe };
   }
   const policy = checkedPolicy(req.policy);
   if (!policy.ok) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: [{ path: 'policy', code: 'invalid-policy', message: policy.message }] };
   const admitted = await admitArtifacts(req);
   if (!admitted.ok) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: admitted.problems };
 
   const capability = capabilityRefusalFor(req.components);
   if (capability !== undefined) return { ok: false, reason: 'refused', at: capability.at, transition: capability.refusal, state: null };
   const remote = remoteEgress(policy.policy, req.components.integrator ?? null);
   if (remote.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: remote };
   const refused = policyRefusal(req.requestedLevel, admitted.admitted.graph, policy.policy);
   if (refused !== undefined) return refused;
+  const rolesAt = await rolesProblems(req);
+  if (rolesAt.length > 0) return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: rolesAt };
+  const roles = await admitRoles(req.roles, admitted.admitted.graph);
+  if (!roles.ok) return roles;
   const worstCase = worstCaseCost(admitted.admitted.graph, policy.policy);
   if (req.requestedLevel > 0 && req.approvedCostUsd !== worstCase.usd) {
     const likely = await likelyCost(req.components, worstCase, policy.policy);
     return { ok: false, reason: 'cost-unapproved', requestedLevel: req.requestedLevel, worstCase, likely, approvedCostUsd: req.approvedCostUsd };
   }
 
   const { vault } = req.components;
   if (await hasState(vault, req.runId)) {
     return {
       ok: false,
       reason: 'invalid-request',
       problems: [{ path: 'runId', code: 'already-admitted', message: `run ${req.runId} was admitted before; resume it, do not start it again` }],
     };
   }
 
   // The base is what every workspace is built from, so it is taken before anything is recorded,
   // and discarded if admission refuses after it: a refusal leaves nothing behind.
   const { workspaces } = req.components;
   const baseTreeSha256 = await snapshotBase(workspaces, req.runId, req.workspace);
   const unbound = await suiteBindingProblems(admitted.admitted.checks, basePath(workspaces, req.runId), req.artifacts.acceptanceTests);
   if (unbound.length > 0) {
     await discardRun(workspaces, req.runId);
     return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: unbound };
   }
   // The security checks the base's stack must be scanned with, read from the base like the suite bindings (R17).
   const set = await adapterSetOf(basePath(workspaces, req.runId), req.components);
   const unscanned = securityProblems(admitted.admitted.checks, { secretScan: req.components.scanners.secretScan, staticAnalysis: set.staticAnalysis });
   if (unscanned.length > 0) {
     await discardRun(workspaces, req.runId);
     return { ok: false, reason: 'invalid-request', problems: unscanned };
   }
   const controls = unavailableControls(set, req.requestedLevel);
 
   const now = new Date().toISOString();
   const run: Run = {
     id: req.runId,
     repo: req.workspace,
     baseCommit: req.baseCommit,
     trigger: { kind: 'human', eventId: req.runId, lineage: { depth: 0, chain: [], windowStart: now } },
     requestedLevel: req.requestedLevel,
     station: 'intake',
     graph: null,
     createdAt: now,
   };

     run,
     policy: policy.policy,
+    roles: roles.roles,
     artifacts: admitted.admitted.artifacts,
     baseTreeSha256,
     unavailableControls: controls,
     // The same detection the controls and the security checks were read from (R18).
     stack: set.stack,
   };
   const refusedControls = admissionRefusal(record);
   if (refusedControls !== undefined) {
     await discardRun(workspaces, req.runId);
     return refusedControls;

       admission,
       station: 'intake',
       phase: 'working',
       tasks: {},
       attempts: {},
       results: {},
       evidenceRefs: [],
       violations: [],
       approvals: [],
       reviews: [],

       cancelled: null,
       halted: null,

 /** A record's `specParts`: an object whose security part is an admitted artifact or null. Read from a record, so its type is not trusted. */

 /**
  * The admission record, read back and checked in full: a record that does not
  * hold what the line reads is refused, not trusted (D-P1-12).
  */
 async function readAdmission(vault: Vault, state: RunState): Promise<{ record: AdmissionRecord; policy: Policy }> {
   const parsed = parseJson(await vault.read(state.admission));
   const r = (typeof parsed === 'object' && parsed !== null ? parsed : {}) as Partial<Record<keyof AdmissionRecord, unknown>>;
   const run = r.run as Partial<Run> | undefined;
   const artifacts = r.artifacts as Partial<AdmissionRecord['artifacts']> | undefined;
   const shaped =
     typeof run === 'object' && run.id === state.runId && typeof run.repo === 'string' && typeof run.baseCommit === 'string' &&
     typeof run.requestedLevel === 'number' && typeof artifacts === 'object' &&
     Array.isArray(artifacts.spec) && Array.isArray(artifacts.acceptanceTests) &&
     typeof artifacts.verificationManifest === 'object' && typeof artifacts.taskGraph === 'object' &&
     specPartsShaped(artifacts.specParts) &&
     typeof r.baseTreeSha256 === 'string' && Array.isArray(r.unavailableControls) &&
     r.unavailableControls.every((control) => typeof control === 'string') &&
     // Absent on a run admitted before R18, which resumes as it did; present, it is a stack.
     (r.stack === undefined || (typeof r.stack === 'string' && r.stack !== ''));
   if (!shaped) throw new Error(`run ${state.runId}: the admission record does not hold a run and its artifacts; refusing to resume from it`);
+  // A run admitted before R20 pinned no roles, and its seats have none to run under; it is not resumed under today's files (D-A-RC-06).
+  const roles = rolesProblem(r.roles);
+  if (roles !== undefined) throw new Error(`run ${state.runId}: ${roles}; refusing to resume from it`);
   const policy = checkedPolicy(r.policy);
   if (!policy.ok) throw new Error(`run ${state.runId}: the admitted policy no longer validates; refusing to resume under it:\n${policy.message}`);
   return { record: r as AdmissionRecord, policy: policy.policy };
 }
 
+/**
+ * Why a record's `roles` is not a role set a resume can run, or undefined:
+ * each role's hash is its instructions' hash, so the record holds the
+ * versions it names, and `reviewer` is among them.
+ */
+function rolesProblem(roles: unknown): string | undefined {
+  if (!Array.isArray(roles)) return 'the admission record pins no roles';
+  for (const role of roles as unknown[]) {
+    const r = (typeof role === 'object' && role !== null ? role : {}) as Partial<Record<keyof CompiledRole, unknown>>;
+    if (typeof r.role !== 'string' || typeof r.instructions !== 'string' || typeof r.sha256 !== 'string') return 'a pinned role carries no id, instructions, and hash';
+    if (createHash('sha256').update(r.instructions, 'utf8').digest('hex') !== r.sha256) return `the pinned role ${r.role}'s instructions do not hash to its version`;
+  }
+  if (!(roles as Array<{ role?: unknown }>).some((r) => r.role === REVIEWER_ROLE)) return 'the admission record pins no reviewer role';
+  return undefined;
+}

@@ -709,64 +800,65 @@ async function lostReadings(vault: Vault, state: RunState): Promise<TaskId[]> {
 export async function resumeRun(req: ResumeRequest, hooks: DriveHooks = {}): Promise<RunOutcome> {
   requireContractTable();
   const { vault } = req.components;
   const state = await vault.readRunState(req.runId);
   const { record, policy } = await readAdmission(vault, state);
   const level = record.run.requestedLevel;
 
   // Admission's own checks, repeated: a refusal here is recorded as a resume refusal, by admission (D-P14-11).
   const resumeRefused = async (refusal: ResumeRefusal): Promise<void> => {
     await recordDecision({ vault, runId: req.runId, taskId: null, station: null }, { cause: 'resume-refused', decidedBy: 'admission', refusal });
   };
   const unsafe = unsafeComponents(req.components);
   if (unsafe.length > 0 && level > 1) {
     await resumeRefused({ reason: 'unsafe-above-l1', requestedLevel: level, components: unsafe.map((u) => u.component) });
     return { ok: false, reason: 'unsafe-above-l1', requestedLevel: level, unsafe };
   }
   // A call whose cost was lost is not replayed on top of it: a human cancels the run (D-P13-20, D-I1a-08).
   const lost = await lostReadings(vault, state);
   if (lost.length > 0) {
     await resumeRefused({ reason: 'meter-lost', tasks: lost });
     return { ok: false, reason: 'meter-lost', tasks: lost, state };
   }
   // Not recorded: admission refuses this record before any state exists, so no run reaches a resume with it.
   const controls = admissionRefusal(record);
   if (controls !== undefined) return controls;
   const capability = capabilityRefusalFor(req.components);
   if (capability !== undefined) {
     await resumeRefused({ reason: 'refused', at: capability.at, refusal: capability.refusal });
     return { ok: false, reason: 'refused', at: capability.at, transition: capability.refusal, state };
   }
   // The base every workspace is built from must be the one admission recorded. A store that lost
   // it, or holds another run's, is refused rather than built on.
   const base = await treeDigest(basePath(req.components.workspaces, req.runId)).catch(() => 'missing');
   if (base !== record.baseTreeSha256) {
     throw new Error(`run ${req.runId}: the workspace store's base is ${base}, not the ${record.baseTreeSha256} admission recorded; refusing to resume on it`);
   }
 
   const reloaded = await reloadExecuted(record);
   const ctx: LineContext = {
     run: record.run,
     policy,
+    roles: record.roles,
     artifacts: record.artifacts,
     graph: reloaded.graph,
     checks: reloaded.checks,
     securityPart: reloaded.securityPart,
     components: req.components,
     baseTreeSha256: record.baseTreeSha256,
     state,
     ...hooks,
   };
   if (reloaded.changed.length > 0) return runLine(ctx, reloaded.changed);
   return runLine(ctx);
 }
 
 /**
  * Records a human's approval of the exit the run is waiting at, and nothing
  * else. The key must be exactly the station the run is exiting and the level
  * it was admitted at, and the effective approval there must be
  * `human-required`: a `blocked` exit cannot be approved, an `auto` one needs no
  * approval, and a grant for any other station or level would be a standing
  * approval of something nobody has seen yet. A grant already recorded and not
  * yet spent is not duplicated; one already spent does not stand in for the
  * visit now waiting, so a rebuild is approved in its own right (A-P4-04).
  */

 /**
  * `driver` doing the hello fixture's work: each builder task writes
  * `hello.txt` through the sandbox it was handed, as an agent would, and
  * claims it, so the fixture's reproduction passes over the change after
  * failing over the base. Every other call goes to `driver` unchanged.
  * Wrapping a wrapped driver replaces its sandbox rather than adding a second
  * write, so a test that swaps the graph's sandbox writes through the new one.
  */
 export function doingTheWork<D extends Driver>(driver: D, sandbox: SandboxProvider): D {
   const inner = (UNWRAPPED.get(driver) ?? driver) as D;
   const wrapped = new Proxy(inner, {
     get(target, key, receiver) {
       if (key !== 'runTask') return Reflect.get(target, key, receiver) as unknown;
       return async (req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> => {
-        if (req.role !== BUILDER) return target.runTask(req);
+        if (req.role.role !== BUILDER) return target.runTask(req);
         const wrote = await sandbox.exec(req.sandbox, ['node', '-e', `require('node:fs').writeFileSync(${JSON.stringify(HELLO_FILE)}, 'hello')`]);
         if (wrote.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`the hello fixture's work did not land in task ${req.taskId}: ${wrote.stderr}`);
         const result = await target.runTask(req);
         if (result.claim.filesChanged.includes(HELLO_FILE)) return result;
         return { ...result, claim: { ...result.claim, filesChanged: [...result.claim.filesChanged, HELLO_FILE] } };
       };
     },
   });
   UNWRAPPED.set(wrapped, inner);
   return wrapped;
 }

   test('a sandbox whose cost cannot be read after the driver ran records the call as lost and stops the run, and a resume over it is refused and recorded', async () => {
     // A driver call's sandbox, the one writable sandbox, is the one whose meter is lost; the base run's check sandbox is read (D-A-BF-01).
     class UnreadableMeter extends DelegatingSandbox {
       private readonly writable = new Set<SandboxHandle>();
 
       override async provision(spec: SandboxSpec): Promise<SandboxHandle> {
         const handle = await this.inner.provision(spec);
         if (spec.mounts.workspace.mode === 'rw') this.writable.add(handle);
         return handle;
       }
 
       override async destroy(h: SandboxHandle): Promise<Teardown> {
         const teardown = await this.inner.destroy(h);
         if (!this.writable.has(h)) return teardown;
         throw new Error("the model relay's meter could not be read");
       }
     }

   test('a process stop while a driver call is in flight leaves a pending record, so the resume is refused and the total is a lower bound (D-I1a-12)', async () => {
     // Nothing reaches the Vault once the process has stopped: every write after the call began is lost with it.
     class StopsMidCall extends DelegatingVault {
       stopped = false;
       private dead<T>(write: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
         return this.stopped ? Promise.reject(new Error('the process stopped')) : write();
       }
       override recordUsage(r: UsageRecord): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.recordUsage(r));
       }
       override recordDecision(d: EnforcementDecision): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.recordDecision(d));
       }
       override recordTaskResult(id: RunId, r: TaskResult): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.recordTaskResult(id, r));
       }
       override writeEvidence(b: EvidenceBundle): Promise<VaultRef> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.writeEvidence(b));
       }
       override commitRunState(s: RunState, ifVersion: string): Promise<RunState> {
         return this.dead(() => this.inner.commitRunState(s, ifVersion));
       }
     }
     const vault = new StopsMidCall(components.vault);
     class StopsTheProcess extends ChosenDriver {
       override runTask(req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> {
         this.requests.push(req);
         vault.stopped = true;
         return Promise.reject(new Error('the process stopped during the call'));
       }
     }
     const killed = new StopsTheProcess({ narrative: 'built' });
     await expect(startRun(runRequest(runId, workspace, { ...components, vault, driver: killed }))).rejects.toThrow(/the process stopped/u);
     expect(killed.requests).toHaveLength(1);
 
     const state = await components.vault.readRunState(runId);
     const totals = costTotals(await readUsage(components.vault, state));
     expect(totals.run).toMatchObject({ calls: 1, lost: 1, bound: 'lower' });
 
     const driver = new ChosenDriver({ narrative: 'built' });
     expect(await resumeRun({ runId, components: built({ ...components, driver }) })).toMatchObject({ ok: false, reason: 'meter-lost', tasks: [hello] });
     expect(driver.requests).toHaveLength(0);
   });

     { id: 'hello-review', station: 'review', role: 'reviewer', dependsOn: ['hello'], dependencySet: ['**'] },

   const driver: Driver = Object.assign(Object.create(stub) as Driver, {

   url = await server.listen(0, '127.0.0.1');
 });

===== packages/compiler/roles/builder.md =====
# Builder

You build one task of a run. The message you are given is the task's id. The
task graph in your context names that task, the station it belongs to, and
the tasks it depends on.

## What you work from

Your context holds the locked spec, the acceptance tests, and the task graph.
The spec says what to build. The acceptance tests are how the change is
judged. Both were fixed before you started, and neither is yours to change.

The repository is in your working directory. Make the change there.

## What you must not do

- Do not edit the spec, the acceptance tests, the verification manifest, or
  the task graph, wherever they sit in the tree. A change to any of them is
  recorded as tampering and the task fails.
- Do not weaken, skip, delete, or regenerate a test to make it pass. Each of
  those is recorded as a finding and put in front of the person who approves
  the change.
- Do not change files outside what your task needs. A change outside the paths
  your role is granted is recorded as a violation and the run stops.
- Do not try to reach anything the session does not offer you. Tools and
  network access you were not given are unavailable by design.

## How you are judged

The runtime collects the change you leave in the tree and runs the declared
checks over it, in a sandbox you cannot reach. The result of those checks
decides the task. Nothing you say decides it.

## What to say when you finish

Your final message is recorded as your account of the work, and it is compared
with the change the runtime collected. Say what you changed and why, and say
plainly what you did not finish or could not make work. Do not describe work
you did not do.

===== packages/compiler/roles/reviewer.md =====
# Reviewer

You review the change a run's build produced. The message you are given is the
id of the review task.

## What you work from

Your context holds the locked spec, the acceptance tests, the list of paths
the change touched, and the evidence the runtime collected for each reviewed
task: what the declared checks found over the base and over the change. The
working directory holds the repository with the change applied. It is
read-only: you cannot change anything, and you are not asked to.

## What to check

- Does the change do what the spec asks, and nothing the spec does not ask?
- Do the acceptance tests exercise the behavior the spec describes, and does
  the evidence show them failing before the change and passing after it?
- Is there anything in the change that makes a check pass without the behavior
  being there: a test weakened or skipped, a check bypassed, a special case
  for the test's input, a suppressed warning?
- Is there anything the change breaks that no check covers?

You are not shown what the author said about its work. Judge the change by
the code and the evidence.

## What to say

Your final message is your review. It is recorded beside the evidence for the
person who approves the change. For each problem, name the file and what is
wrong with it. If you found nothing wrong, say so and say what you checked. If
you could not review something, say what and why. Do not soften a finding and
do not invent one.

===== packages/compiler/src/compile.ts =====
/**
 * Compiles the roles directory into the instructions each seat runs under
 * (D-A-RC-01, D-A-RC-02).
 *
 * A role is one file, `<role-id>.md`, whose whole content is its
 * instructions. There is no front matter, so nothing in a role file can be
 * read as a grant, a model, or a tier: what a role may do comes from policy
 * through `TaskRequest` and nowhere else (I4). The role's version is the
 * SHA-256 of the file's bytes as read.
 *
 * The only input is a directory, and only the bytes of the files in it are
 * read. No parameter exists through which an `UntrustedPayload`, an extracted
 * trigger field, a rubric, an acceptance check, or a corpus could arrive, so
 * untrusted text never reaches a role (I7) and nothing that judges an agent
 * is written into its instructions (I3, D-A-RC-05).
 *
 * Every limit is a refusal that names the entry, never a skip or a truncation
 * (I5): an entry that is not a regular file, a name that is not a role id, a
 * file over the byte cap (checked before a byte is decoded), bytes that are
 * not UTF-8, and a file with no instructions in it.
 */
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { constants, type BigIntStats } from 'node:fs';
import { lstat, open, readdir, stat, type FileHandle } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { CompiledRole, RoleId } from '@olympus-ai/core';

/**
 * The most bytes one role file may hold. A role reaches the CLI as one argv
 * element, JSON-escaped, and Linux refuses a single argument past 128 KiB;
 * half that leaves room for the escaping a prose file needs (D-R20-03).
 */
export const ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES = 32 * 1024;

/**
 * A role id as a file name can carry it: lowercase ASCII words joined by
 * single hyphens, at most 64 characters. Lowercase only, so two roles cannot
 * collide on a filesystem that ignores case (D-R20-02).
 */
const ROLE_ID = /^[a-z][a-z0-9]*(?:-[a-z0-9]+)*$/;
const ROLE_ID_MAX_LENGTH = 64;
const EXTENSION = '.md';

/** Whether `name` is a role id a role file can be named for. */
export function isRoleId(name: string): name is RoleId {
  return name.length <= ROLE_ID_MAX_LENGTH && ROLE_ID.test(name);
}

export type RoleFileCode =
  /** The directory, or an entry in it, could not be read. */
  | 'unreadable'
  /** A directory, a symbolic link, or anything else that is not a regular file. */
  | 'not-regular'
  /** Not `<role-id>.md`. */
  | 'invalid-name'
  /** Past `ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES`. */
  | 'too-large'
  | 'not-utf8'
  /** No bytes, or nothing but whitespace: instructions of nothing. */
  | 'empty';

/**
 * One entry the compiler refused. `entry` is its name inside the directory,
 * or `.` for the directory itself.
 */
export interface RoleFileRefusal {
  readonly entry: string;
  readonly code: RoleFileCode;
  readonly message: string;
}

/** Every role in the directory, sorted by id, or every entry refused. Never some of each. */
export type RoleCompilation =
  | { readonly ok: true; readonly roles: readonly CompiledRole[] }
  | { readonly ok: false; readonly refused: readonly [RoleFileRefusal, ...RoleFileRefusal[]] };

type EntryOutcome = { readonly ok: true; readonly role: CompiledRole } | { readonly ok: false; readonly refusal: RoleFileRefusal };

function refused(entry: string, code: RoleFileCode, message: string): { ok: false; refusal: RoleFileRefusal } {
  return { ok: false, refusal: { entry, code, message } };
}

function describe(error: unknown): string {
  return error instanceof Error ? error.message : String(error);
}

/** Follows no link where the platform can refuse one at open; elsewhere the identity check below catches a swap. */
// eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-condition -- typed as a number, and undefined at run time on Windows, which has no such flag.
const OPEN_FLAGS = constants.O_RDONLY | (constants.O_NOFOLLOW ?? 0);

/** At most `max + 1` bytes: enough to tell a file at the cap from one past it, and never the whole of an oversized one. */
async function readCapped(handle: FileHandle, max: number): Promise<Uint8Array> {
  const buffer = new Uint8Array(max + 1);
  let filled = 0;
  while (filled < buffer.byteLength) {
    const { bytesRead } = await handle.read(buffer, filled, buffer.byteLength - filled, null);
    if (bytesRead === 0) break;
    filled += bytesRead;
  }
  return buffer.subarray(0, filled);
}

function sameFile(a: BigIntStats, b: BigIntStats): boolean {
  return a.dev === b.dev && a.ino === b.ino;
}

async function compileEntry(directory: string, entry: string): Promise<EntryOutcome> {
  const path = join(directory, entry);
  let listed: BigIntStats;
  try {
    listed = await lstat(path, { bigint: true });
  } catch (error) {
    return refused(entry, 'unreadable', `role entry '${entry}' could not be read: ${describe(error)}`);
  }
  // A link is refused whatever it points at: one into a workspace would hand a role to the agent it instructs (I1).
  if (!listed.isFile()) return refused(entry, 'not-regular', `role entry '${entry}' is not a regular file; the roles directory holds only <role-id>.md files`);
  if (!entry.endsWith(EXTENSION) || !isRoleId(entry.slice(0, -EXTENSION.length))) {
    return refused(entry, 'invalid-name', `role file '${entry}' is not named <role-id>.md, where a role id is lowercase words joined by hyphens, at most ${String(ROLE_ID_MAX_LENGTH)} characters`);
  }
  const role = entry.slice(0, -EXTENSION.length) as RoleId;
  let bytes: Uint8Array;
  let handle: FileHandle;
  try {
    handle = await open(path, OPEN_FLAGS);
  } catch (error) {
    // ELOOP is the platform refusing a link that replaced the file after it was listed.
    const code = (error as { code?: unknown }).code === 'ELOOP' ? 'not-regular' : 'unreadable';
    return refused(entry, code, `role file '${entry}' could not be opened: ${describe(error)}`);
  }
  try {
    // The file opened is the file listed: a swap between the two is refused, not followed.
    const opened = await handle.stat({ bigint: true });
    if (!opened.isFile() || !sameFile(listed, opened)) {
      return refused(entry, 'not-regular', `role file '${entry}' changed between being listed and being opened`);
    }
    if (opened.size > BigInt(ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES)) {
      return refused(entry, 'too-large', `role file '${entry}' is ${String(opened.size)} bytes; the limit is ${String(ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES)}`);
    }
    bytes = await readCapped(handle, ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES);
  } catch (error) {
    return refused(entry, 'unreadable', `role file '${entry}' could not be read: ${describe(error)}`);
  } finally {
    await handle.close();
  }
  // Checked again on what was read, before a byte is decoded: a file that grew after the stat is still refused.
  if (bytes.byteLength > ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES) {
    return refused(entry, 'too-large', `role file '${entry}' is more than ${String(ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES)} bytes, the limit`);
  }
  let instructions: string;
  try {
    // `ignoreBOM` keeps a byte-order mark in the text, so the text encodes back to exactly the bytes hashed.
    instructions = new TextDecoder('utf-8', { fatal: true, ignoreBOM: true }).decode(bytes);
  } catch {
    return refused(entry, 'not-utf8', `role file '${entry}' is not UTF-8`);
  }
  // `trim` removes a byte-order mark with the rest of the whitespace.
  if (instructions.trim() === '') {
    return refused(entry, 'empty', `role file '${entry}' holds no instructions`);
  }
  return { ok: true, role: { role, instructions, sha256: createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex') } };
}

/**
 * Reads and compiles every entry in `directory`. A refused entry refuses the
 * whole directory: a run is never admitted with some of its roles.
 */
export async function compileRoles(directory: string): Promise<RoleCompilation> {
  let entries: string[];
  try {
    if (!(await stat(directory)).isDirectory()) {
      return { ok: false, refused: [{ entry: '.', code: 'unreadable', message: `the roles directory '${directory}' is not a directory` }] };
    }
    entries = await readdir(directory);
  } catch (error) {
    return { ok: false, refused: [{ entry: '.', code: 'unreadable', message: `the roles directory '${directory}' could not be read: ${describe(error)}` }] };
  }
  entries.sort();
  const roles: CompiledRole[] = [];
  const refusals: RoleFileRefusal[] = [];
  for (const entry of entries) {
    const outcome = await compileEntry(directory, entry);
    if (outcome.ok) roles.push(outcome.role);
    else refusals.push(outcome.refusal);
  }
  const [first, ...rest] = refusals;
  if (first !== undefined) return { ok: false, refused: [first, ...rest] };
  return { ok: true, roles };
}

===== packages/compiler/src/index.ts =====
/**
 * The role compiler (R20): turns the roles directory a person wrote into the
 * `CompiledRole`s admission pins, each versioned by the SHA-256 of its file.
 * Depends on core's published types and nothing else.
 */
import { fileURLToPath } from 'node:url';

export {
  compileRoles,
  isRoleId,
  ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES,
  type RoleCompilation,
  type RoleFileCode,
  type RoleFileRefusal,
} from './compile.js';

/**
 * The roles shipped with the runtime, `builder` and `reviewer`: one seat at
 * `build` and the review seat. A service is configured with a roles directory
 * as it is with a policy file; this is one it can be pointed at.
 */
export const DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY: string = fileURLToPath(new URL('../roles/', import.meta.url));

===== packages/compiler/test/compile.test.ts =====
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { mkdir, mkdtemp, readdir, readFile, rm, symlink, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { tmpdir } from 'node:os';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import { afterEach, beforeEach, describe, expect, test } from 'vitest';
import { compileRoles, DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY, isRoleId, ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES, type RoleCompilation } from '../src/index.js';

let dir: string;

beforeEach(async () => {
  dir = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'roles-'));
});

afterEach(async () => {
  await rm(dir, { recursive: true, force: true });
});

function sha256(bytes: Uint8Array | string): string {
  return createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex');
}

function refusedOnly(outcome: RoleCompilation): ReadonlyArray<{ entry: string; code: string }> {
  if (outcome.ok) throw new Error(`expected a refusal, compiled ${outcome.roles.map((r) => r.role).join(', ')}`);
  return outcome.refused.map(({ entry, code }) => ({ entry, code }));
}

describe('compileRoles', () => {
  test('compiles every role file, sorted by id, versioned by the SHA-256 of its bytes', async () => {
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'reviewer.md'), 'Review the change.\n');
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), 'Build the task.\n');
    const outcome = await compileRoles(dir);
    expect(outcome).toEqual({
      ok: true,
      roles: [
        { role: 'builder', instructions: 'Build the task.\n', sha256: sha256('Build the task.\n') },
        { role: 'reviewer', instructions: 'Review the change.\n', sha256: sha256('Review the change.\n') },
      ],
    });
  });

  test('a byte-order mark stays in the instructions, so they encode back to the bytes hashed', async () => {
    const bytes = new Uint8Array([0xef, 0xbb, 0xbf, ...new TextEncoder().encode('Build.')]);
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), bytes);
    const outcome = await compileRoles(dir);
    if (!outcome.ok) throw new Error('refused');
    const [role] = outcome.roles;
    expect(role?.sha256).toBe(sha256(bytes));
    expect(sha256(new TextEncoder().encode(role?.instructions))).toBe(role?.sha256);
  });

  test('front matter is instructions like any other text, never a field', async () => {
    const text = '---\ntools: [Bash]\nmodel: deep\n---\nBuild.\n';
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), text);
    const outcome = await compileRoles(dir);
    if (!outcome.ok) throw new Error('refused');
    expect(Object.keys(outcome.roles[0] ?? {}).sort()).toEqual(['instructions', 'role', 'sha256']);
    expect(outcome.roles[0]?.instructions).toBe(text);
  });

  test('an empty directory compiles to no roles; whether that admits a run is admission\'s call', async () => {
    expect(await compileRoles(dir)).toEqual({ ok: true, roles: [] });
  });

  test('a file exactly at the byte cap compiles, and one byte past it is refused', async () => {
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), 'a'.repeat(ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES));
    expect((await compileRoles(dir)).ok).toBe(true);
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), 'a'.repeat(ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES + 1));
    expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(dir))).toEqual([{ entry: 'builder.md', code: 'too-large' }]);
  });

  test('the cap is checked before decoding: an oversized file that is not UTF-8 is refused as too large', async () => {
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), new Uint8Array(ROLE_FILE_MAX_BYTES + 1).fill(0xff));
    expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(dir))).toEqual([{ entry: 'builder.md', code: 'too-large' }]);
  });

  test('bytes that are not UTF-8 are refused, never replaced', async () => {
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), new Uint8Array([0x42, 0xc3, 0x28]));
    expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(dir))).toEqual([{ entry: 'builder.md', code: 'not-utf8' }]);
  });

  test('an empty file, and one holding only whitespace, are refused', async () => {
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), '');
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'reviewer.md'), '﻿ \n\t\n');
    expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(dir))).toEqual([
      { entry: 'builder.md', code: 'empty' },
      { entry: 'reviewer.md', code: 'empty' },
    ]);
  });

  test.each(['Builder.md', 'builder.txt', 'builder', 'build_er.md', '-builder.md', 'builder-.md', 'a--b.md', '.md', `${'a'.repeat(65)}.md`, '.hidden.md', 'builder.MD'])(
    'a file named %j is refused as misnamed',
    async (name) => {
      await writeFile(join(dir, name), 'Build.');
      expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(dir))).toEqual([{ entry: name, code: 'invalid-name' }]);
    },
  );

  test('a directory in the roles directory is refused, not descended into', async () => {
    await mkdir(join(dir, 'nested.md'));
    expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(dir))).toEqual([{ entry: 'nested.md', code: 'not-regular' }]);
  });

  test('a symbolic link is refused even when it points at a valid role file', async (context) => {
    const elsewhere = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'roles-target-'));
    try {
      await writeFile(join(elsewhere, 'builder.md'), 'Build.');
      try {
        await symlink(join(elsewhere, 'builder.md'), join(dir, 'builder.md'), 'file');
      } catch {
        context.skip('this host cannot create a symbolic link without privilege');
      }
      expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(dir))).toEqual([{ entry: 'builder.md', code: 'not-regular' }]);
    } finally {
      await rm(elsewhere, { recursive: true, force: true });
    }
  });

  test('every refused entry is named, and a refusal compiles no role at all', async () => {
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), 'Build.');
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'notes.txt'), 'x');
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'reviewer.md'), '');
    const outcome = await compileRoles(dir);
    expect(refusedOnly(outcome)).toEqual([
      { entry: 'notes.txt', code: 'invalid-name' },
      { entry: 'reviewer.md', code: 'empty' },
    ]);
    expect(outcome).not.toHaveProperty('roles');
  });

  test('a missing directory, and a file where the directory should be, are refused as unreadable', async () => {
    expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(join(dir, 'absent')))).toEqual([{ entry: '.', code: 'unreadable' }]);
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'file'), 'x');
    expect(refusedOnly(await compileRoles(join(dir, 'file')))).toEqual([{ entry: '.', code: 'unreadable' }]);
  });

  test('each refusal message names the entry', async () => {
    await writeFile(join(dir, 'Builder.md'), 'Build.');
    const outcome = await compileRoles(dir);
    if (outcome.ok) throw new Error('compiled');
    expect(outcome.refused[0].message).toContain('Builder.md');
  });
});

describe('isRoleId', () => {
  test.each(['builder', 'reviewer', 'spec-drafter', 'a1', 'a'.repeat(64)])('%j is a role id', (id) => {
    expect(isRoleId(id)).toBe(true);
  });
  test.each(['', 'Builder', '1builder', 'a_b', 'a--b', 'a-', '__proto__', 'a'.repeat(65)])('%j is not', (id) => {
    expect(isRoleId(id)).toBe(false);
  });
});

describe('the shipped roles', () => {
  test('are builder and reviewer, and compile', async () => {
    expect((await readdir(DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY)).sort()).toEqual(['builder.md', 'reviewer.md']);
    const outcome = await compileRoles(DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY);
    if (!outcome.ok) throw new Error(JSON.stringify(outcome.refused));
    expect(outcome.roles.map((r) => r.role)).toEqual(['builder', 'reviewer']);
    for (const role of outcome.roles) {
      expect(role.sha256).toBe(sha256(await readFile(join(DEFAULT_ROLES_DIRECTORY, `${role.role}.md`))));
    }
  });
});

===== packages/conformance/fixtures/types/i7/compiler-refuses-untrusted-input.ts =====
// I7 and I3: the compiler's only input is the roles directory. No parameter
// exists through which an UntrustedPayload, its raw text, or anything that
// judges an agent could reach a role's instructions (D-A-RC-05).
import { compileRoles } from '@olympus-ai/compiler';
import type { CheckSpec } from '@olympus-ai/integrity';
import type { UntrustedPayload } from '@olympus-ai/triggers';

declare const payload: UntrustedPayload;
declare const check: CheckSpec;

export const fromPayload = compileRoles(payload); // expect-error TS2345: Argument of type 'UntrustedPayload' is not assignable to parameter of type 'string'
export const fromRaw = compileRoles(payload.raw); // expect-error TS2345: Argument of type 'UntrustedText' is not assignable to parameter of type 'string'
export const fromCheck = compileRoles(check); // expect-error TS2345: is not assignable to parameter of type 'string'
export const withPayload = compileRoles('/roles', payload); // expect-error TS2554: Expected 1 arguments, but got 2
export const fromDirectory = compileRoles('/roles');

===== packages/conformance/src/registry/base-run.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/conformance/src/registry/base-run.ts b/packages/conformance/src/registry/base-run.ts
index ea8109d..f2b9811 100644
--- a/packages/conformance/src/registry/base-run.ts
+++ b/packages/conformance/src/registry/base-run.ts
@@ -402,17 +402,17 @@ export const SUITE_CHECK_RUNS_WHAT_IT_NAMES: LocalAssertion = runtime({
 /** The rig's stub builder, made to write one file under node_modules besides whatever the rig has it do. */
 async function writingDependency(path: string, text: string, sandbox: SandboxProvider): Promise<Driver> {
   const driver = await stubDriver();
   return new Proxy(driver, {
     get(target, key, receiver) {
       if (key !== 'runTask') return Reflect.get(target, key, receiver) as unknown;
       return async (req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> => {
-        if ((req.role as string) === 'builder') {
+        if (req.role.role === 'builder') {
           const script = `const fs = require('node:fs'); fs.mkdirSync(require('node:path').dirname(${JSON.stringify(path)}), { recursive: true }); fs.writeFileSync(${JSON.stringify(path)}, ${JSON.stringify(text)})`;
           const wrote = await sandbox.exec(req.sandbox, ['node', '-e', script]);
           if (wrote.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`the write under node_modules did not land: ${wrote.stderr}`);
         }
         return target.runTask(req);
       };
     },
   });
 }

   'resume:meter-lost': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       // A call whose sandbox could not be read stops the run with the reading recorded lost; the resume is refused for it (D-P13-20).
       await withLine('p14-meter-lost-', async (rig) => {
         const { resumeRun, startRun } = await api();
         const base = await rig.components();
         let destroyed = 0;
         const unreadable: SandboxProvider = {
           ...forwarding(base.sandbox),

             const teardown = await base.sandbox.destroy(h);
             destroyed += 1;
             // The base run's check sandbox is torn down first; the driver's call is the one whose meter is lost.
             if (destroyed === DRIVER_CALL) throw new Error("the relay's meter could not be read");
             return teardown;
           },
         };
         await startRun(await rig.request({ ...base, sandbox: unreadable })).then(
           () => { throw new Error('P14: a run whose meter could not be read was not stopped'); },
           (error: unknown) => { if (!String(error).includes('could not be destroyed')) throw error; },
         );

   'relay-refused': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-relay-', async (rig) => {
         const refusing: MeterReading = { kind: 'metered', calls: 3, inputTokens: 300, outputTokens: 30, cacheReadTokens: 0, cacheWriteTokens: 0, costUsd: 1, exhausted: 'cost', refused: 2 };
         const sandbox = await reportingProvider((n) => (n === DRIVER_CALL ? { meter: refusing } : {}));
         const components = await rig.components({ sandbox });
         await stoppedForApproval(rig, components);
         const [usage] = (await rig.state()).usage;
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, components.vault, 'relay-refused', { decidedBy: 'model-relay', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'build' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'relay-refused' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.usage) !== JSON.stringify(usage) || d.decision.refused !== 2 || d.decision.exhausted !== 'cost') {
           throw new Error(`P14: the relay's refusals were recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}`);
         }
         // A call whose relay refused nothing records no relay decision.
         const relayed = (await decisionsOf(rig, components.vault)).filter((x) => x.decision.cause === 'relay-refused');
         if (relayed.length !== 1) throw new Error(`P14: ${String(relayed.length)} relay decisions for one refusing call`);
       });
     },
   },
   'task-conflict': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: () => conflictScenario('task-conflict', (decisions) => {
       const found = decisions.filter((d) => d.decision.cause === 'task-conflict');
       const [d] = found;
       // Only the line may record one, which the Vault holds it to (A-R4-03).
       if (found.length !== 1 || d?.decision.cause !== 'task-conflict' || d.taskId !== 'b' || d.station !== 'verify'
         || JSON.stringify(d.decision.with) !== '["a"]' || JSON.stringify(d.decision.paths) !== '["parts/shared.txt"]') {
         throw new Error(`P14: a conflict was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(found)}`);
       }
     }),
   },
   'tier-escalated': {
     kind: 'scenario',
     run: async () => {
       await withLine('p14-escalate-', async (rig) => {
         const run = await failingRun(rig, { tier: 'fast', escalation: { afterFailedGates: 1, ceiling: 'standard' } });
         const vault = (await rig.components()).vault;
         const d = await expectDecision(rig, vault, 'tier-escalated', { decidedBy: 'line', taskId: HELLO_TASK, station: 'build' });
         if (d.decision.cause !== 'tier-escalated' || d.decision.from !== 'fast' || d.decision.to !== 'standard' || d.decision.failedGates !== 1 || run.decisions.length !== 1) {
           throw new Error(`P14: an escalation was recorded as ${JSON.stringify(d.decision)}, among ${String(run.decisions.length)}`);
         }
       });
     },
   },
 };
 
 /** The literals whose `reason` property is declared by `StationRefusal`, found in a package's own source. */
 /**
  * The string a literal's `reason` property is set to, however it is spelled:
  * a quoted key, and a literal inside parentheses, `as`, `satisfies`, or an
  * angle-bracket assertion, all count (external review of P14, codex-7).
  */

       run: async () => {
         await withVault('p1-i3-change-', async (vault, dirs) => {
           const runId = 'i3-change' as RunId;
           const spec = join(dirs.artifacts, 'spec.md');
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           const manifest = await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'spec');
           const locked = entryFor(manifest.entries, 'spec.md');
 
           const intact = await vault.verifyLocks(runId);
           if (!intact.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I3: an untouched locked artifact verified as tampered (${JSON.stringify(intact.tampered)})`);
           }
 
           // The failure this invariant exists for: the artifact an agent is
           // judged by is edited after it was locked.

           const changed = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a rewritten locked artifact');
           if (changed.path !== 'spec.md') throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record named ${changed.path}, expected spec.md`);
           if (changed.expected !== locked.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record's expected hash is ${changed.expected}, not the ${locked.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
           if (changed.actual === changed.expected) {
             throw new Error('I3: the tampered record reports the same hash for expected and actual, so nothing was re-read from disk');
           }

           // record the violation and refuse, exactly as it does for an edit.
           await rm(spec);
           const deleted = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a deleted locked artifact');

             throw new Error('I3: a deleted locked artifact reports the same actual hash as an edited one; a deletion must be distinguishable');
           }
           if (deleted.expected !== locked.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the deleted artifact's expected hash is ${deleted.expected}, not the ${locked.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),

       id: 'I3.lock-preserves-earlier-entries',

       run: async () => {
         await withVault('p1-i3-preserve-', async (vault, dirs) => {
           const runId = 'i3-preserve' as RunId;
           const spec = join(dirs.artifacts, 'spec.md');
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
           await writeFile(join(dirs.artifacts, 'acceptance.test.ts'), "test('hello', () => { expect(run()).toBe(0); });\n");
 
           const first = await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'spec');
           const lockedSpec = entryFor(first.entries, 'spec.md');
 
           // The second station locks its own artifact. Replace semantics would
           // drop the spec here, and I3 requires both locked and re-verified at
           // every transition.
           const second = await vault.lock(runId, ['acceptance.test.ts'], 'test-design');
           const preserved = entryFor(second.entries, 'spec.md');
           entryFor(second.entries, 'acceptance.test.ts');
           if (preserved.sha256 !== lockedSpec.sha256) {

           }

           }
 
           // Verification after the second lock must still cover the first's entries.
           await writeFile(spec, '# hello\n\nThe capability, quietly widened.\n');
           const tampered = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a spec edited after a later station locked its own artifact');
           if (tampered.path !== 'spec.md') {
             throw new Error(`I3: after the second lock, verification named ${tampered.path}; the spec locked at \`spec\` is no longer covered`);
           }
 
           // Re-locking is how a tamper would launder itself: lock the edited
           // file again and the manifest agrees with the disk.
           let rebased = true;
           try {
             await vault.lock(runId, ['spec.md'], 'test-design');
           } catch {
             rebased = false;
           }
           if (rebased) throw new Error('I3: an already-locked artifact was locked again, which rebases its hash onto the edited bytes');
           const after = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), 'a locked artifact after a refused re-lock');

             throw new Error(`I3: the refused re-lock still moved the manifest's hash to ${after.expected}`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),

       run: async () => {

           const runId = 'i3-escape' as RunId;
           const inside = join(dirs.artifacts, 'sub');
           const locked = join('sub', 'spec.md');
           await mkdir(inside, { recursive: true });
           await writeFile(join(inside, 'spec.md'), '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           const manifest = await vault.lock(runId, [locked], 'spec');
           const entry = entryFor(manifest.entries, locked);
           const before = await vault.verifyLocks(runId);
           if (!before.ok) throw new Error(`I3: the artifact verified as tampered before anything was substituted (${JSON.stringify(before.tampered)})`);
 
           // Identical bytes at the far end. Nothing but the escape
           // distinguishes this from an intact file, which is the whole point:
           // a check that compares content alone cannot see this.
           const elsewhere = join(base, 'elsewhere');
           await mkdir(elsewhere, { recursive: true });
           await writeFile(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'), '# hello\n\nThe capability, as locked.\n');
 
           // No try/catch: a platform whose mechanism will not build fails here.
           if (ESCAPE_MECHANISM === 'junction') {
             await rm(inside, { recursive: true });
             await symlink(elsewhere, inside, 'junction');
           } else {
             await rm(join(inside, 'spec.md'));
             await symlink(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'), join(inside, 'spec.md'));
           }
 
           // The escape is real before the Vault is asked about it, so the
           // verdict below cannot pass for the wrong reason.
           const reached = await realpath(join(dirs.artifacts, locked));
           const target = await realpath(join(elsewhere, 'spec.md'));
           if (reached !== target) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM} did not take effect on ${process.platform}: ${locked} still resolves to ${reached}`);
           }
 
           const escaped = soleTampered(await vault.verifyLocks(runId), `a locked artifact substituted via a ${ESCAPE_MECHANISM}`);
           if (escaped.path !== entry.path) throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record named ${escaped.path}, expected ${entry.path}`);
           if (escaped.expected !== entry.sha256) {
             throw new Error(`I3: the tampered record's expected hash is ${escaped.expected}, not the ${entry.sha256} the manifest locked`);
           }
           if (escaped.actual !== 'escaped') {
             throw new Error(
               `I3: a locked artifact that resolves outside the artifact root reported actual '${escaped.actual}'. ` +
                 "The bytes are identical, so anything but 'escaped' means the link was followed and the substitution was hashed clean.",
             );
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     TRANSITION_REVERIFIES_LOCKS,
     STATION_LOCKS_THE_ADMITTED_ARTIFACT,
     LOCKED_PATH_IN_DIFF_IS_TAMPER,
     // P7: tamper analysis over the trees a task was handed and verified in.
     CASE_SET_REDUCTION_IS_A_DELETION,
     TAMPER_FINDING_ESCALATES_INTEGRATE,
     SKIP_MARKER_IS_A_FINDING,
     CHECK_DISPATCH_NOT_WRITABLE_BY_THE_TASK,
     // I1a: on the composed line, a task that rewrites a locked test fails the run.
     COMPOSED_HOST_LOCKED_TEST_CHANGE_FAILS,
     // R15: an acceptance check that passes on the base is a gate the change could not fail.
     PASSING_ON_THE_BASE_REFUSES,
     DEPENDENCY_TREES_ARE_THE_BASES,
   ],
   pending: [
     pending({
       id: 'I3.coverage-report-is-not-writable-by-the-suite',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         'Changed-line coverage is read from the istanbul report a check run writes, and that run executes the '
         + "repository's own code: its tests, and whatever its package scripts do around them. So the artifact the "
         + 'coverage number is derived from is one the agent being judged can write. P8 closed the half of this that '
         + 'parsing can close — the report can no longer decide which lines count, only which of them ran, because '
         + "the denominator is now the union of the report's statements and the executable lines read from the head "
         + 'tree on the host (external review, codex-2 and gemini-2). The other half cannot be closed by reading the '
         + 'report more carefully: a hit count claiming a line ran cannot be told apart from one that did, by anyone '
         + 'who was not there when it ran. Closing it means the run that produces the report: coverage '
         + 'collected where the suite cannot rewrite it afterwards, or counted from an instrumentation the repository '
         + 'does not supply, with an assertion that a forged report does not raise the number. Until then a coverage '
         + 'figure is evidence about a suite that cooperated, not proof against one that did not. Recorded by P8 as '
         + 'D-P8-14. Owned by M3, beside mutation testing: the suite and the coverage writer share a process, so no '
         + 'location for the report decides whether its hit counts are true (D-P6-05).',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I3.unchanged-assertion-still-judges',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         'Tamper analysis reads the test files that changed and compares what they say. An assertion it reads as '
         + 'unchanged can stop judging without its file changing, or while the change reads as nothing: a helper, fixture, '
         + 'custom matcher, setup file, or type the test imports is rewritten to pass everything; the assertion is moved '
         + 'into a branch that never runs or a function never called; a script the pinned command runs imports a module '
         + 'that now does nothing. None of those is a test file, and reachability is not decidable by parsing. The '
         + "policy's protected paths can name the files a repository knows to be load-bearing, and a pinned command's "
         + 'own script is protected (D-P7-10), but no reading of the diff decides what an unchanged assertion now '
         + 'executes. Closing it means running the suite against mutants of the code under test and requiring the '
         + 'suite that passed at the base to kill the ones it killed there, with an assertion that a helper rewritten to '
         + 'return early, and an assertion made unreachable, each lower the kill rate and escalate. Raised by the P7 '
         + 'external review (codex-1, gemini-4, gemini-5) and recorded as D-P7-11. Owned by M3, beside mutation testing.',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I3.runner-process-loads-only-base-code',
       owner: 'M3',
       reason:
         "A suite check runs the base's installed test framework, and nothing a task writes under node_modules is "
         + "carried (D-R15-05), but the runner's own process can still run code the change can reach. The installed "
         + 'runner is not authenticated: admission checks only that its file exists, and the suite count comes from '
         + "the adapter's static selection model, not from the process that exited, so a fabricated launcher that "
         + 'exits on a flag file fails on the base and passes at verify with no test run (codex-1). And an honest '
         + "vitest loads files outside node_modules into its own process: a globalSetup file the base's config "
         + 'already names, rewritten to call process.exit(0), makes vitest 4.1.11 exit 0 with no test run, and it is '

         + 'change cannot write: provisioned by the runtime outside the tree, or authenticated and held to the base. '
         + 'That needs an assertion that each route fails the check. Tracked as issue #42. Owned by M3, beside the '
         + 'L3 canary, which needs the same trusted runner.',
     }),
   ],
 };

     runtime({
       id: 'I5.stale-commit-is-refused-under-contention',
       title:
         'concurrent commitRunState calls from separate processes against one ifVersion leave exactly one winner holding the whole record; every loser is refused with a reason rather than silently applied, and a stale commit afterwards stores nothing',
       run: async () => {
         await withVaultDirs('p1-i5-contend-', async (dirs, base) => {
           const runId = 'i5-contend';
           const outcomes = await contendOnCommit(dirs, base, runId, '0', CONTENDERS);
 
           const winners = outcomes.filter((o) => o.ok);
           if (winners.length !== 1) {

             throw new Error(
               `I5: ${String(CONTENDERS)} processes committed run state from version 0 and ${String(winners.length)} succeeded; ` +

             );

           if (winner === undefined) throw new Error('I5: the winner list has one entry and no entry in it');
           if (winner.version !== '1') {
             throw new Error(`I5: the winning commit stored version ${String(winner.version)}, expected 1`);
           }
           for (const loser of outcomes.filter((o) => !o.ok)) {
             if (loser.message === '') {
               throw new Error(`I5: the refused commit from ${loser.tag} carries no reason; a refusal that says nothing is a silent failure`);
             }
           }

           // blend: each child tags its own `tasks`, so the stored state names
           // exactly one of them.
           const { LocalVault } = await import('@olympus-ai/vault');
           const vault = new LocalVault(dirs);
           const stored = await vault.readRunState(runId as RunId);
           if (stored.version !== '1') throw new Error(`I5: the stored state is at version ${stored.version}, expected 1`);
           const tags = Object.keys(stored.tasks);
           if (tags.length !== 1 || tags[0] !== winner.tag) {
             throw new Error(`I5: the stored state carries tasks [${tags.join(', ')}]; expected only the winner's ${winner.tag}`);
           }
 
           // The same refusal, uncontended: a commit from a version that has
           // already been superseded stores nothing at all.
           let stale = true;

             await vault.commitRunState({ ...stored, version: '0' }, '0');

           const after = await vault.readRunState(runId as RunId);
           if (after.version !== '1' || Object.keys(after.tasks).join(',') !== winner.tag) {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refused stale commit changed the store to version ${after.version} with tasks [${Object.keys(after.tasks).join(', ')}]`);
           }
         });
