     runtime({
       id: 'I5.over-request-refused',
       title:
         'the real engine refuses a level above the global cap, the station cap, or the role ceiling with reason exceeds-cap, carries no level on the refusal, and grants a request at or below the tightest bound at exactly the level asked for',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(grantingDocument());
 
         // Three bounds, three different values, so each refusal names the one
         // that actually bit. `build` is capped below the role ceiling, which is
         // itself below the global cap.
         const overRequests: Array<{ readonly level: AutonomyLevel; readonly station: StationId; readonly bound: string }> = [
           { level: 2, station: 'build', bound: `the ${String(BUILD_CAP)} cap on build` },
           { level: 3, station: 'verify', bound: `the role ceiling of ${String(ROLE_CEILING)}` },
         ];
         for (const { level, station, bound } of overRequests) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, station, GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           if (outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(
               `I5: L${String(level)} at '${station}' was granted at L${String(outcome.level)} against ${bound}. ` +
                 'An over-request must be refused; a downgrade returns a level the caller never asked for and did not ' +
                 'agree to, while reporting success.',
             );
           }
           if (outcome.reason !== 'exceeds-cap') {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at '${station}' was refused as '${outcome.reason}'; expected 'exceeds-cap'`);
           }
           if (Object.hasOwn(outcome, 'level')) {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries a level; there must be nothing on it to mistake for a grant`);
           }
           if (outcome.detail === '') {
             throw new Error(`I5: the refusal for L${String(level)} at '${station}' carries no detail`);
           }
         }
 
         // The global cap, which the fixture document sets to the maximum and
         // so can never bite on its own. Lowered here, it must refuse a request
         // the station cap and role ceiling would both have allowed.
         const grounded = engine.resolvePolicy({ ...grantingDocument(), globalCap: 0, stationCaps: {} });
         const global = engine.resolveAutonomy(1, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded);
         if (global.ok) {
           throw new Error(`I5: L1 at 'verify' was granted at L${String(global.level)} under a global cap of L0`);
         }
         if (global.reason !== 'exceeds-cap') {
           throw new Error(`I5: a request above the global cap was refused as '${global.reason}'; expected 'exceeds-cap'`);
         }
         if (!global.detail.includes('global L0')) {
           throw new Error(`I5: the refusal does not name the bound that bit: ${global.detail}`);
         }
         if (!engine.resolveAutonomy(0, 'verify', GRANTED_ROLE, grounded).ok) {
           throw new Error('I5: L0 under a global cap of L0 was refused; the cap is a ceiling, not an exclusion');
         }
 
         // The control: a request within every bound is granted, and granted at
         // the level requested rather than raised or lowered to a cap. An engine
         // that refused everything would satisfy the loop above.
         for (const level of [0, 1] as const) {
           const outcome = engine.resolveAutonomy(level, 'build', GRANTED_ROLE, policy);
           if (!outcome.ok) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' is within every bound and was refused (${outcome.reason}: ${outcome.detail})`);
           }
           if (outcome.level !== level) {
             throw new Error(`I5: L${String(level)} at 'build' resolved to L${String(outcome.level)}; a request inside the caps is granted as asked, never adjusted`);
           }
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-document-refused-at-resolve',
       title:
         'resolvePolicy refuses a value that does not validate as a PolicyDocument, even though the parameter type says it is one, so a caller that asserts past the compiler cannot resolve a grant out of a malformed document; a genuine document still resolves',
       run: async () => {
         const { StrictPolicyEngine } = await import('@olympus-ai/core');
         const engine = new StrictPolicyEngine();
         const document = grantingDocument();
         const scope = document.roles[GRANTED_ROLE];
         if (scope === undefined) throw new Error('I5: the fixture document defines no granted role');
 
         // Each of these is what a caller produces by writing `value as
         // PolicyDocument`. Without the guard, the first resolves a global cap
         // of 99 and grants every level; the rest widen a scope the document
         // never authorised.
         const rogues: Array<{ readonly what: string; readonly value: unknown }> = [
           { what: 'a global cap of 99', value: { ...document, globalCap: 99 } },
           { what: 'a role ceiling of 99', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, autonomyCeiling: 99 } } } },
           { what: 'a station id that is not one of the ten', value: { ...document, roles: { [GRANTED_ROLE]: { ...scope, stations: ['deploy'] } } } },
           { what: 'an unknown top-level key', value: { ...document, backdoor: true } },
         ];
         for (const { what, value } of rogues) {
           let refused = false;
           try {
             engine.resolvePolicy(value as never);
           } catch {
             refused = true;
           }
           if (!refused) {
             throw new Error(
               `I5: resolvePolicy accepted ${what}. The parameter type is a compiler claim, and a control that holds ` +
                 'only while every caller remembers to validate first is not closed.',
             );
           }
         }
 
         // The control: a genuine document resolves. A guard that threw on
         // everything would satisfy the loop above and break the engine.
         const policy = engine.resolvePolicy(document);
         if (policy.globalCap !== document.globalCap) {
           throw new Error(`I5: a valid document resolved to globalCap ${String(policy.globalCap)}, expected ${String(document.globalCap)}`);
         }
       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.malformed-autonomy-level-refused',
       title:
         'resolveAutonomy refuses a requested level that is not one of the four, rather than comparing it against the cap: NaN, a negative, and a fraction all survive a numeric comparison and would otherwise be answered with success carrying a level that is not a level',
       run: async () => {

             refused = true;

         }

         for (const level of AUTONOMY_LEVELS) {

           const allowed = level <= ROLE_CEILING;

             throw new Error(`I5: level ${String(level)} at 'verify' was ${outcome.ok ? 'granted' : 'refused'}, expected the opposite`);
           }

       },
     }),
     runtime({
       id: 'I5.sandbox-egress-allowlist-enforced',
       title:
         'a sandbox provisioned with egress mode allowlist reaches the one host it names and no other, by name and by address; ' +
         'the direct route does not exist, so unsetting every proxy variable reaches nothing; an empty allow list is refused rather ' +
         'than read as deny-all or allow-all; and deny-all is unchanged, with --network none, no proxy, and no network created',
       run: async () => {
         await withProvider('i5-egress-allowlist-', async (provider, dirs) => {
           // An allowlist that names nothing is refused. It is not deny-all wearing the wrong
           // name, and it is not permission to reach everything; either reading would be the
           // silent degrade this invariant exists to refuse (D-P2-07, reversed by P10).
           const empty = await refusalFrom(() => provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [] } })));
           if (empty.layer !== 'egress') {
             throw new Error(`I5: an empty allowlist was refused at the ${empty.layer} layer, not the egress layer`);
           }
 
           // deny-all is untouched by the allowlist existing: no proxy is started beside a
           // sandbox entitled to no egress, and no network is created for it.
           await withSandbox(provider, specFor(dirs), async (handle) => {
             const controls = provider.appliedControls(handle);
             if (controls.egress.mode !== 'deny-all') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all spec was applied as ${controls.egress.mode}`);
             }
             // `network: 'none'` is the type's guarantee once the mode is deny-all, so what is
             // read here is what Docker was actually told and what the container actually got.
             if (!controls.runArgs.join(' ').includes('--network none')) {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox was not started with --network none: ${controls.runArgs.join(' ')}`);
             }
             const interfaces = await provider.exec(handle, ['ls', '/sys/class/net']);
             if (interfaces.stdout.trim().split(/\s+/).join(',') !== 'lo') {
               throw new Error(`I5: a deny-all sandbox has interfaces [${interfaces.stdout.trim()}]; expected loopback alone`);
             }
           });
 
           const name = originName();
           const handle = await provider.provision(specFor(dirs, 'rw', { egress: { mode: 'allowlist', allow: [name] } }));
           const { egress } = allowlistControls(provider, handle);
           try {
             await withOrigin(egress.proxy.outboundNetwork, name, async (origin) => {
               // The grant. The body proves the origin answered, not the proxy on its behalf.
               const reached = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c', `wget -T 8 -O - http://${origin.name}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (!reached.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was not reached: ${reached.stdout}${reached.stderr}`);
               }
 
               // Refused by name, and by address. The second is the one that matters: a refusal
               // that only ever fires on a name is a DNS failure wearing an allowlist's clothes.
               for (const host of [BLOCKED_NAME, BLOCKED_ADDRESS]) {
                 const answer = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${host}/ HTTP/1.0`);
                 if (!answer.includes('403') || !answer.includes(host)) {
                   throw new Error(`I5: ${host} is not on the allowlist and was not refused by name in the answer: ${answer}`);
                 }
               }
 
               // The strongest form: the origin's own address, which the proxy demonstrably
               // reaches, refused for the one reason under test — the address is not on the list.
               const sameHost = await throughProxy(provider, handle, `GET http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ HTTP/1.0`);
               if (!sameHost.includes('403') || sameHost.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: a reachable host was allowed by address although only its name was granted: ${sameHost}`);
               }
 
               // There is nothing to bypass. The internal network carries no default route, so a
               // process that unsets every proxy variable is left with no second path to take.
               const routes = await provider.exec(handle, ['sh', '-c', 'ip route']);
               if (routes.stdout.includes('default')) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the sandbox has a default route, so the proxy is a convention rather than the only way out: ${routes.stdout}`);
               }
               const bypass = await provider.exec(handle, [
                 'sh', '-c',
                 'unset http_proxy https_proxy HTTP_PROXY HTTPS_PROXY no_proxy NO_PROXY; ' +
                   `wget -T 4 -O - http://${origin.address}:${String(ORIGIN_PORT)}/ 2>&1`,
               ]);
               if (bypass.exitCode === 0 || bypass.stdout.includes(ORIGIN_BODY)) {
                 throw new Error(`I5: the allowlisted host was reached with every proxy variable unset: ${bypass.stdout}`);
               }
             });
           } finally {
             await provider.destroy(handle);
           }
 
           // The proxy is the sandbox's, and goes with it. One left running is a route off the
           // host with no workload behind it and nobody watching it.
           for (const [kind, leaked] of [
             ['container', egress.proxy.name],
             ['network', egress.proxy.internalNetwork],
             ['network', egress.proxy.outboundNetwork],
           ] as const) {
             if (await dockerHas(kind, leaked)) throw new Error(`I5: the ${kind} ${leaked} outlived the sandbox it served`);
           }
         });
       },
     }),
     STATION_MISSING_CAPABILITY_REFUSED,
     TASK_ATTEMPTS_ARE_BOUNDED,
     OVER_REQUEST_REFUSED_AT_ADMISSION,
     UNSUPPORTED_STACK_IS_LOUD,
     // P11: a provider with no probe is named, never trusted to observe the product from inside it.
     HTTP_PROBE_ABSENT_IS_NAMED,
     ADAPTER_REFUSAL_ENFORCED_AT_ADMISSION,
     CHECK_COMMAND_HAS_A_GRAMMAR,
     MISSING_CHECK_OR_SHRUNKEN_SUITE_REFUSES,
     WORKSPACE_IS_WRITABLE_BY_THE_TASK,
     // P13: what the relay cannot count, it charges or refuses; it never forwards it free.
     MODEL_RELAY_FAILS_CLOSED_ON_UNMETERED_USAGE,
     // I1a: what the walking skeleton's own declaration stood in for, each now refusing on its own.
     UNSAFE_DECLARATION_SURVIVES_COMPOSITION,
     ADAPTER_REFUSAL_REFUSES_L3_END_TO_END,
     UNANALYSED_TESTS_ESCALATE_THE_APPROVAL,

       id: 'I5.unanalysed-tests-are-not-reported-clean',
       title:
         'an L2 run on the composed host over a repository with no vitest or jest stops at the integrate approval for the unanalysed suite, which escalates an exit that would otherwise advance; paid, one build and one review call',
       level: 'runtime',
       package: '@olympus-ai/api',
       file: 'test/paid/line.paid.test.ts',
     }),
     // R15: no default declaration, nothing to reproduce is refused, and a check that did not start is not a failure.
     BASE_DECLARATION_HAS_NO_DEFAULT,
     NOTHING_FAILING_ON_THE_BASE_IS_REFUSED,
     UNSTARTED_BASE_CHECK_REFUSES,
     SUITE_CHECK_RUNS_WHAT_IT_NAMES,
   ],
   pending: [],
 };

         const hits = inlineSuppressions(I7_LINT_RULES, workspacePackages());
         if (hits.length > 0) {
           const detail = hits.map((h) => `${h.file}:${String(h.line)}: ${h.text}`).join('\n  ');
           throw new Error(`I7: a lint rule the invariant depends on is suppressed inline\n  ${detail}`);
         }
       },
     }),
   ],
   pending: [
     pending({
       id: 'I7.enabled-trigger-declares-a-template',
       owner: 'M2',
       reason:
         'TriggerPolicy validates its fields independently, not relationally: `enabled: ["ci-failure"]` with empty '
         + '`taskTemplate`, `entryStation`, `maxAutonomy` and `minAuthorTrust` maps is a valid document, and the '
         + 'shipped default itself enables `human` with an empty `taskTemplate` (D-P3-05, deliberately, because no '
         + 'template registry exists yet). I7 holds only while a trigger selects a pre-declared template, so an '
         + 'enabled kind with no declared template is a hole for admission to fill permissively — the payload is then '
         + 'the only thing left that could name one. Trigger admission is M2 and out of scope for P3, so the '
         + 'obligation lands there: refuse to admit an enabled kind that has no pre-declared template and no entry '
         + 'station, and assert the refusal. Surfaced by the second P3 external review, finding 5. Whether the '
         + 'relational check belongs in the validator instead is recorded in D-P3-13.',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I7.extracted-field-schemas',
       owner: 'M2',
       reason:
         'TriggerEvent.extracted is the only path from a payload into a run and it is an unconstrained ' +
         'Record<string, string>. Per-kind field schemas with length caps and character-class validation ' +
         'must exist, and be asserted, before any non-human trigger is enabled. The trigger framework is M2.',
     }),
     pending({
       id: 'I7.untrusted-sink-lint-rule',
       owner: 'M2',

 /**
  * `driver` doing the hello fixture's work: each builder task writes
  * `hello.txt` through the sandbox it was handed, as an agent would, and
  * claims it. The fixture's acceptance check fails on the base and passes over
  * this change, so a run whose builder changed nothing parks at `verify`
  * rather than passing a gate the change never had to meet. Every other call
  * goes to `driver` unchanged, so what an assertion observes of it holds.
  */
 export function doingTheWork<D extends Driver>(driver: D, sandbox: SandboxProvider): D {
   return new Proxy(driver, {
     get(target, key, receiver) {
       if (key !== 'runTask') return Reflect.get(target, key, receiver) as unknown;
       return async (req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> => {
-        if (req.role === ('builder' as RoleId)) {
+        if (req.role.role === ('builder' as RoleId)) {
           const wrote = await sandbox.exec(req.sandbox, ['node', '-e', `require('node:fs').writeFileSync(${JSON.stringify(HELLO_FILE)}, 'hello')`]);
           if (wrote.exitCode !== 0) throw new Error(`the hello fixture's work did not land in task ${req.taskId}: ${wrote.stderr}`);
         }
         const result = await target.runTask(req);
-        if (req.role !== ('builder' as RoleId) || result.claim.filesChanged.includes(HELLO_FILE)) return result;
+        if (req.role.role !== ('builder' as RoleId) || result.claim.filesChanged.includes(HELLO_FILE)) return result;
         return { ...result, claim: { ...result.claim, filesChanged: [...result.claim.filesChanged, HELLO_FILE] } };
       };
     },
   });
 }

===== packages/conformance/src/registry/parallel.ts =====
diff --git a/packages/conformance/src/registry/parallel.ts b/packages/conformance/src/registry/parallel.ts

--- a/packages/conformance/src/registry/parallel.ts

@@ -68,41 +68,41 @@ interface Overlap {
 /**
  * A stub declaring `parallelism`, whose builder calls each write
  * `parts/<task>.txt` as `good` (or what `write` says) and hold the call open
  * for `holdMs`, counted in `overlap`. With `meet`, a call that has written
  * first waits until `meet` calls have written or `MEET_MS` passes, so whether
  * calls overlap is decided by the line and not by how fast a loaded host
  * reaches each one. `during` runs after that.
  */
 async function wideDriver(
   sandbox: SandboxProvider,
   parallelism: number,
   options: { holdMs?: number; meet?: number; overlap?: Overlap; write?: (req: TaskRequest, call: number) => Record<string, string> | null; during?: DriverOptions['during'] } = {},
 ): Promise<ObservedDriver> {
   let inside = 0;
   let written = 0;
   const calls = new Map<TaskId, number>();
   return stubDriver({
     capabilities: { parallelism },
     during: async (req) => {
-      if (req.role !== ('builder' as TaskRequest['role'])) return;
+      if (req.role.role !== 'builder') return;
       const call = (calls.get(req.taskId) ?? 0) + 1;
       calls.set(req.taskId, call);
       inside += 1;
       let wrote = false;
       if (options.overlap !== undefined) options.overlap.mostAtOnce = Math.max(options.overlap.mostAtOnce, inside);
       try {
         const files = options.write === undefined ? { [`parts/${req.taskId}.txt`]: 'good' } : options.write(req, call);
         if (files !== null) await inTask(sandbox, req, writes(files));
         written += 1;
         wrote = true;
         const until = Date.now() + MEET_MS;
         while (options.meet !== undefined && written < options.meet && Date.now() < until) await delay(10);
         if (options.during !== undefined) await options.during(req);
         await delay(options.holdMs ?? 150);
       } finally {
         inside -= 1;
         if (wrote) written -= 1;
       }
     },
   });
 }

 * R20: the role compiler. Every seat runs under instructions a person wrote,
 * compiled from the roles directory at admission and pinned there by hash
 * (D-A-RC-01 to D-A-RC-06).
 *
 * Each assertion drives the real admission and line in `@olympus-ai/api` over
 * the real compiler and filesystem Vault, with the rig's copy of the shipped
 * roles, so a role file edited or broken here is one the runtime reads. The
 * Claude Code driver's rendering is checked on the argv it builds, over a
 * provider that records every exec and starts nothing.
 */
import { createHash } from 'node:crypto';
import { cp, mkdir, readdir, readFile, rm, symlink, writeFile } from 'node:fs/promises';
import { join } from 'node:path';
import type { RunOutcome } from '@olympus-ai/api';
import type { CompiledRole, RoleId, TaskId, TaskRequest } from '@olympus-ai/core';
import type { ExecResult, SandboxHandle, SandboxProvider, SandboxSpec, Teardown } from '@olympus-ai/sandbox';
import type { AdmissionRecord, UsageRecord } from '@olympus-ai/vault';
import { compileError, runtime } from '../kit/assert.js';
import type { LocalAssertion } from '../kit/types.js';
import { workspaceRoot } from '../kit/workspace.js';
import { HELLO_REVIEW, HELLO_TASK, lineScope, linePolicy, readRecord, stubDriver, withLine, type LineRig } from './line.js';

const api = async () => import('@olympus-ai/api');

function sha256(bytes: Uint8Array | string): string {
  return createHash('sha256').update(bytes).digest('hex');
}

/** The hash of the rig's copy of a role file, as it stands now. */
async function fileHash(rig: LineRig, role: string): Promise<string> {
  return sha256(await readFile(join(rig.roles, `${role}.md`)));
}

/** A run of the hello fixture to its stop for approval at `integrate`, through a stub driver that records every request. */
async function runHello(rig: LineRig, beforeDrive?: () => Promise<void>) {
  const { admitRun } = await api();
  const driver = await stubDriver();
  const reviewer = await stubDriver({ family: 'other' });
  const components = await rig.components({ driver, reviewer });
  const admission = await admitRun(await rig.request(components));
  if (!admission.ok) throw new Error(`R20: the hello run was refused at admission as ${admission.reason}`);
  if (beforeDrive !== undefined) await beforeDrive();
  const outcome = await admission.admitted.drive();
  return { outcome, driver, reviewer, components };
}

async function admissionOf(rig: LineRig, vault: Parameters<typeof readRecord>[0]): Promise<AdmissionRecord> {
  const state = await vault.readRunState(rig.runId);
  return readRecord<AdmissionRecord>(vault, state.admission);
}

/** The refusal `outcome` carries, or a failure naming what it was instead. */
function refusedAs<R extends Exclude<RunOutcome, { ok: true }>['reason']>(outcome: RunOutcome, reason: R, what: string): Extract<RunOutcome, { reason: R }> {
  if (outcome.ok || outcome.reason !== reason) throw new Error(`R20: ${what} was ${outcome.ok ? 'admitted' : `refused as ${outcome.reason}`}, not refused as ${reason}`);
  return outcome as Extract<RunOutcome, { reason: R }>;
}

/** Nothing ran and nothing but the decision was stored: no state, and no driver call. */
async function nothingRan(rig: LineRig, requests: readonly unknown[], what: string): Promise<void> {
  if (requests.length > 0) throw new Error(`R20: ${what} reached the driver ${String(requests.length)} times before it was refused`);
  const { isNoRunState } = await import('@olympus-ai/vault');
  const vault = (await rig.components()).vault;
  try {
    await vault.readRunState(rig.runId);
  } catch (error) {
    if (isNoRunState(error)) return;
    throw error;
  }
  throw new Error(`R20: ${what} left run state behind`);
}

export const ADMISSION_PINS_EVERY_ROLE: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I3.admission-pins-every-role',
  title:
    'a run admitted with the roles directory records every compiled role, its instructions, and the SHA-256 of its file in the admission record; a role file edited after admission changes nothing the run\'s tasks receive, before the drive or on a resume, while a later run gets the edit',
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r20-pin-', async (rig) => {
      const builderHash = await fileHash(rig, 'builder');
      const original = await readFile(join(rig.roles, 'builder.md'), 'utf8');
      const edited = `${original}\nAn instruction added after admission.\n`;
      const { driver, components } = await runHello(rig, () => writeFile(join(rig.roles, 'builder.md'), edited));

      const record = await admissionOf(rig, components.vault);
      const pinned = record.roles.map((r) => ({ role: r.role, sha256: r.sha256 }));
      const expected = [{ role: 'builder', sha256: builderHash }, { role: 'reviewer', sha256: await fileHash(rig, 'reviewer') }];
      if (JSON.stringify(pinned) !== JSON.stringify(expected)) throw new Error(`R20: the admission record pins ${JSON.stringify(pinned)}, not every role in the directory with its file's hash ${JSON.stringify(expected)}`);
      if (record.roles[0]?.instructions !== original) throw new Error('R20: the admission record does not carry the builder role\'s instructions as admitted');

      const [build] = driver.requests;
      if (build === undefined) throw new Error('R20: the builder was never called');
      if (build.role.instructions !== original || build.role.sha256 !== builderHash) {
        throw new Error('R20: the builder ran under the role file as edited after admission, not as the run was admitted with');
      }

      // A resume reads the pinned roles from the record, never the directory.
      const { resumeRun } = await api();
      const again = await stubDriver();
      await resumeRun({ runId: rig.runId, components: await rig.components({ driver: again, reviewer: await stubDriver({ family: 'other' }) }) });
      if (again.requests.some((r) => r.role.instructions !== original && r.role.role === 'builder')) throw new Error('R20: a resume ran the builder under the edited role file');

      // A later run is admitted with the edit.
      const { compileRoles } = await import('@olympus-ai/compiler');
      const later = await compileRoles(rig.roles);
      if (!later.ok || later.roles.find((r) => r.role === 'builder')?.sha256 !== sha256(edited)) throw new Error('R20: the edited role file does not compile to its new version');
    });
  },
});

export const SEAT_WITHOUT_A_ROLE_IS_REFUSED: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.seat-without-a-role-is-refused',
  title:
    'a task graph naming a role with no role file is refused at admission as role-undefined, naming the role, before any driver call and with no run state; so is a run whose roles directory defines no reviewer; the CLI\'s default prompt is never a fallback',
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r20-undefined-', async (rig) => {
      const { startRun } = await api();
      // The graph's builder task names a role the directory lacks, though the policy grants it.
      const graphPath = join(rig.dirs.artifacts, 'graph.json');
      await writeFile(graphPath, (await readFile(graphPath, 'utf8')).replace('"role": "builder"', '"role": "implementer"'));
      const policy = await linePolicy({}, { roles: { ['implementer' as RoleId]: lineScope(['build', 'verify'], 'standard'), ['reviewer' as RoleId]: lineScope(['review'], 'deep') } });
      const driver = await stubDriver();
      const outcome = await startRun(await rig.request(await rig.components({ driver, reviewer: driver }), { policy }));
      const refused = refusedAs(outcome, 'role-undefined', 'a graph naming a role with no file');
      if (JSON.stringify(refused.roles) !== JSON.stringify(['implementer'])) throw new Error(`R20: the refusal named ${JSON.stringify(refused.roles)}, not the undefined role`);
      await nothingRan(rig, driver.requests, 'a graph naming a role with no file');
    });
    await withLine('r20-no-reviewer-', async (rig) => {
      const { startRun } = await api();
      await rm(join(rig.roles, 'reviewer.md'));
      const driver = await stubDriver();
      const outcome = await startRun(await rig.request(await rig.components({ driver, reviewer: driver })));
      const refused = refusedAs(outcome, 'role-undefined', 'a run with no reviewer role');
      if (!refused.roles.includes('reviewer' as RoleId)) throw new Error(`R20: the refusal named ${JSON.stringify(refused.roles)}, not reviewer`);
      await nothingRan(rig, driver.requests, 'a run with no reviewer role');
    });
  },
});

/** One broken entry, and the code the compiler must refuse it with. */
const BROKEN_ENTRIES: ReadonlyArray<{ readonly what: string; readonly entry: string; readonly code: string; readonly make: (dir: string) => Promise<void> }> = [
  { what: 'an oversized role file', entry: 'builder.md', code: 'too-large', make: (dir) => writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), 'a'.repeat(32 * 1024 + 1)) },
  { what: 'a role file that is not UTF-8', entry: 'builder.md', code: 'not-utf8', make: (dir) => writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), new Uint8Array([0x42, 0xc3, 0x28])) },
  { what: 'an empty role file', entry: 'builder.md', code: 'empty', make: (dir) => writeFile(join(dir, 'builder.md'), '') },
  { what: 'a misnamed role file', entry: 'Planner.md', code: 'invalid-name', make: (dir) => writeFile(join(dir, 'Planner.md'), 'Plan.') },
  { what: 'a directory in the roles directory', entry: 'drafts', code: 'not-regular', make: (dir) => mkdir(join(dir, 'drafts')) },
];

export const BROKEN_ROLE_ENTRY_REFUSES_ADMISSION: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I5.broken-role-entry-refuses-admission',
  title:
    'an oversized, non-UTF-8, empty, misnamed, or non-regular entry in the roles directory refuses admission as roles-refused, naming the entry and its code, before any driver call and with no run state; the 32 KiB cap admits a file exactly at it; a symbolic link is refused even when it points at a valid role file',
  run: async () => {
    for (const broken of BROKEN_ENTRIES) {
      await withLine(`r20-broken-${broken.code}-`, async (rig) => {
        const { startRun } = await api();
        await broken.make(rig.roles);
        const driver = await stubDriver();
        const outcome = await startRun(await rig.request(await rig.components({ driver, reviewer: driver })));
        const refused = refusedAs(outcome, 'roles-refused', broken.what);
        if (!refused.refused.some((r) => r.entry === broken.entry && r.code === broken.code && r.message.includes(broken.entry))) {
          throw new Error(`R20: ${broken.what} was refused naming ${JSON.stringify(refused.refused)}, not ${broken.entry} as ${broken.code}`);
        }
        await nothingRan(rig, driver.requests, broken.what);
      });
    }
    // The cap is the cap: a file exactly at it is admitted, so the refusal above is the limit's and not the content's.
    await withLine('r20-at-cap-', async (rig) => {
      const { admitRun } = await api();
      await writeFile(join(rig.roles, 'builder.md'), 'a'.repeat(32 * 1024));
      const admitted = await admitRun(await rig.request(await rig.components()));
      if (!admitted.ok) throw new Error(`R20: a role file exactly at the byte cap was refused as ${admitted.reason}`);
    });
    await withLine('r20-link-', async (rig) => {
      const { startRun } = await api();
      const target = join(rig.dirs.artifacts, 'planted-role.md');
      await writeFile(target, 'Instructions planted where an agent can write them.');
      await rm(join(rig.roles, 'builder.md'));
      try {
        await symlink(target, join(rig.roles, 'builder.md'), 'file');
      } catch {
        // A host that cannot make a link without privilege cannot plant one either; the compiler's own suite covers it where it can.
        return;
      }
      const outcome = await startRun(await rig.request(await rig.components()));
      const refused = refusedAs(outcome, 'roles-refused', 'a role file that is a link into the workspace');
      if (!refused.refused.some((r) => r.entry === 'builder.md' && r.code === 'not-regular')) throw new Error(`R20: the link was refused as ${JSON.stringify(refused.refused)}`);
    });
  },
});

export const ROLES_DIRECTORY_OUTSIDE_EVERY_MOUNT: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I1.roles-directory-outside-every-mount',
  title:
    'admission refuses a roles directory inside the workspace or the workspace store, as an invalid request on roles, before anything is compiled or stored: a copy of either is what a sandbox mounts, so a role there is one an agent could rewrite',
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r20-inside-', async (rig) => {
      const { startRun } = await api();
      const places = [join(rig.dirs.artifacts, 'roles'), join(rig.workspaces.root, 'roles')];
      for (const inside of places) {
        await cp(rig.roles, inside, { recursive: true });
        const driver = await stubDriver();
        const outcome = await startRun(await rig.request(await rig.components({ driver, reviewer: driver }), { roles: inside }));
        const refused = refusedAs(outcome, 'invalid-request', `a roles directory at ${inside}`);
        if (!refused.problems.some((p) => p.path === 'roles' && p.code === 'overlaps')) throw new Error(`R20: a roles directory at ${inside} was refused for ${JSON.stringify(refused.problems)}`);
        await nothingRan(rig, driver.requests, `a roles directory at ${inside}`);
        await rm(inside, { recursive: true, force: true });
      }
    });
  },
});

export const ATTEMPT_RECORDS_ITS_ROLE_VERSION: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I2.attempt-records-its-role-version',
  title:
    'every usage record a run state references names the role and the hash of the version its call ran under, beside the model, taken from the admission record: the builder\'s calls name builder, and the review seat\'s names reviewer',
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r20-attempt-', async (rig) => {
      const { components } = await runHello(rig);
      const state = await components.vault.readRunState(rig.runId);
      const record = await admissionOf(rig, components.vault);
      const pinned = new Map(record.roles.map((r) => [r.role as string, r.sha256]));
      const usage = await Promise.all(state.usage.map((ref) => readRecord<UsageRecord>(components.vault, ref)));
      if (usage.length === 0) throw new Error('R20: the run state references no usage record');
      for (const u of usage) {
        const expected = u.taskId === HELLO_REVIEW ? 'reviewer' : u.taskId === HELLO_TASK ? 'builder' : undefined;
        if (u.role.id !== expected || u.role.sha256 !== pinned.get(expected)) {
          throw new Error(`R20: the call for ${u.taskId} records role ${JSON.stringify(u.role)}, not ${String(expected)} at its admitted hash`);
        }
        if (typeof u.model.model !== 'string' || u.model.model === '') throw new Error('R20: a usage record names a role but no model beside it');
      }
      if (!usage.some((u) => u.taskId === HELLO_REVIEW)) throw new Error('R20: no usage record covers the review seat');
    });
  },
});

export const REVIEW_SEAT_RUNS_AS_REVIEWER: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I4.review-seat-runs-as-reviewer',
  title:
    'the review seat runs under the reviewer role\'s pinned instructions whatever role its task names, and under exactly the grants its task\'s role holds at review: a role is instructions, never a grant',
  run: async () => {
    await withLine('r20-review-', async (rig) => {
      // The review task names `auditor`, whose scope at review differs from `reviewer`'s; the seat's instructions are still reviewer's.
      const graphPath = join(rig.dirs.artifacts, 'graph.json');
      await writeFile(graphPath, (await readFile(graphPath, 'utf8')).replace('"role": "reviewer"', '"role": "auditor"'));
      await writeFile(join(rig.roles, 'auditor.md'), 'Instructions no review seat may run under.');
      const auditor = { ...lineScope(['review'], 'deep'), tools: [] as string[] };
      const policy = await linePolicy({}, { roles: { ['builder' as RoleId]: lineScope(['build', 'verify'], 'standard'), ['auditor' as RoleId]: auditor } });
      const { admitRun } = await api();
      const driver = await stubDriver();
      const reviewer = await stubDriver({ family: 'other' });
      const admission = await admitRun(await rig.request(await rig.components({ driver, reviewer }), { policy }));
      if (!admission.ok) throw new Error(`R20: the run was refused at admission as ${admission.reason}`);
      await admission.admitted.drive();
      const seat = reviewer.requests.find((r) => r.taskId === HELLO_REVIEW);
      if (seat === undefined) throw new Error('R20: the review seat was never called');
      const reviewerFile = await readFile(join(rig.roles, 'reviewer.md'), 'utf8');
      if (seat.role.role !== 'reviewer' || seat.role.instructions !== reviewerFile) throw new Error(`R20: the review seat ran under ${seat.role.role}, not the reviewer role`);
      if (JSON.stringify(seat.tools) !== JSON.stringify(auditor.tools)) throw new Error(`R20: the review seat's tools are ${JSON.stringify(seat.tools)}, not its task's grant ${JSON.stringify(auditor.tools)}`);
    });
  },
});

/** Records every exec and starts nothing; answers a `claude` invocation with a session that loaded `agents`. */
class RecordingProvider implements SandboxProvider {
  readonly id = 'recording';
  readonly calls: Array<{ readonly cmd: readonly string[]; readonly env: Readonly<Record<string, string>> }> = [];
  constructor(private readonly key: string, private readonly agents: readonly string[]) {}
  provision(_spec: SandboxSpec): Promise<SandboxHandle> {
    return Promise.resolve('recorded' as SandboxHandle);
  }
  exec(_h: SandboxHandle, cmd: string[], options?: { env?: Readonly<Record<string, string>> }): Promise<ExecResult> {
    this.calls.push({ cmd: [...cmd], env: { ...options?.env } });
    const init = { type: 'system', subtype: 'init', session_id: '11111111-2222-5333-a444-555566667777', tools: ['Read'], mcp_servers: [], agents: this.agents, model: 'm', apiKeySource: this.key, claude_code_version: '0' };
    const result = { type: 'result', subtype: 'success', is_error: false, num_turns: 1, result: 'done', total_cost_usd: 0, duration_ms: 1, usage: { input_tokens: 1, output_tokens: 1 } };
    return Promise.resolve({ exitCode: 0, stdout: `${JSON.stringify(init)}\n${JSON.stringify(result)}`, stderr: '', durationMs: 1 });
  }
  destroy(_h: SandboxHandle): Promise<Teardown> {
    return Promise.resolve({ meter: { kind: 'unmetered' }, egress: { kind: 'none' } });
  }
  capabilities() {
    return { computerUse: false, gpu: false, os: 'linux' as const, persistent: false, remote: false };
  }
}

export const DRIVER_RENDERS_THE_ROLE_ON_THE_INVOCATION: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I3.driver-renders-the-role-on-the-invocation',
  title:
    'the Claude Code driver\'s argv carries the role\'s instructions as the agent the session runs as, with no field beside them that could carry a grant; no exec writes them into the workspace; and a session that did not load the role is refused rather than run under the CLI\'s default prompt',
  run: async () => {
    const { ClaudeCodeDriver, KEY_VARIABLE } = await import('@olympus-ai/driver-claude-code');
    const role: CompiledRole = { role: 'builder' as RoleId, instructions: 'A distinctive line only the role carries.', sha256: sha256('A distinctive line only the role carries.') };
    const request = (sandbox: SandboxHandle): TaskRequest => ({
      taskId: 't' as TaskId, role, stablePrefix: 'granted context', variableSuffix: 't', tier: 'standard', tools: ['Read'], sandbox, timeoutMs: 1000,
      budget: { maxTokens: 1, maxCostUsd: 1, maxWallClockMs: 1000 },
    });
    const provider = new RecordingProvider(KEY_VARIABLE, ['claude', 'builder']);
    await new ClaudeCodeDriver({ provider }).runTask(request('recorded' as SandboxHandle));
    const carrying = provider.calls.filter((c) => [...c.cmd, ...Object.values(c.env)].some((part) => part.includes(role.instructions)));
    const [task] = carrying;
    if (carrying.length !== 1 || !task?.cmd.includes('claude')) throw new Error(`R20: ${String(carrying.length)} execs carry the role's text; exactly one, the CLI invocation, may`);
    const at = task.cmd.indexOf('--agents');
    const agents = JSON.parse(task.cmd[at + 1] ?? '{}') as Record<string, Record<string, unknown>>;
    if (JSON.stringify(agents) !== JSON.stringify({ builder: { description: 'the builder role', prompt: role.instructions } })) {
      throw new Error(`R20: the argv's agent definition is ${JSON.stringify(agents)}, not the role's instructions alone`);
    }
    if (task.cmd[task.cmd.indexOf('--agent') + 1] !== 'builder') throw new Error('R20: the session does not run as the role');

    const unloaded = new RecordingProvider(KEY_VARIABLE, ['claude']);
    try {
      await new ClaudeCodeDriver({ provider: unloaded }).runTask(request('recorded' as SandboxHandle));
    } catch (error) {
      if ((error as Error).message.includes('did not load the builder role')) return;
      throw error;
    }
    throw new Error('R20: a session that did not load its role returned a result');
  },
});

export const COMPILER_TAKES_ONLY_A_DIRECTORY: LocalAssertion = runtime({
  id: 'I7.compiler-takes-only-a-directory',
  title:
    'the compiler package depends on core alone and imports nothing but node built-ins and core, so no trigger payload, check, rubric, or corpus type can reach it; compileRoles takes one parameter',
  run: async () => {
    const root = join(workspaceRoot(), 'packages', 'compiler');
    const manifest = JSON.parse(await readFile(join(root, 'package.json'), 'utf8')) as { dependencies?: Record<string, string> };
    if (JSON.stringify(Object.keys(manifest.dependencies ?? {})) !== JSON.stringify(['@olympus-ai/core'])) {
      throw new Error(`R20: the compiler depends on ${JSON.stringify(manifest.dependencies)}, not on core alone`);
    }
    for (const file of await readdir(join(root, 'src'))) {
      const source = await readFile(join(root, 'src', file), 'utf8');
      for (const [, from] of source.matchAll(/from '([^']+)'/g)) {
        if (from === undefined || from.startsWith('node:') || from.startsWith('./') || from === '@olympus-ai/core') continue;
        throw new Error(`R20: compiler/src/${file} imports ${from}`);
      }
    }
    const { compileRoles } = await import('@olympus-ai/compiler');
    if (compileRoles.length !== 1) throw new Error(`R20: compileRoles takes ${String(compileRoles.length)} parameters, not the directory alone`);
  },
});

export const R20_I5: readonly LocalAssertion[] = [SEAT_WITHOUT_A_ROLE_IS_REFUSED, BROKEN_ROLE_ENTRY_REFUSES_ADMISSION];

  COMPILER_TAKES_ONLY_A_DIRECTORY,
  compileError({
    id: 'I7.compiler-refuses-untrusted-input',
    title: 'compileRoles given an UntrustedPayload, its raw text, or any second argument does not compile: the directory is its only input',
    fixture: 'i7/compiler-refuses-untrusted-input.ts',
  }),
];

 /**
  * ClaudeCodeDriver: the `Driver` contract over the Claude Code CLI, run inside
  * a provisioned sandbox.
  *
  * The whole of `I1.driver-executes-inside-the-sandbox` is that this class
  * holds a `SandboxProvider` and has no other way to start a process. There is
  * no `spawn` in this file, no shell on the host, and no path that runs the CLI
  * outside a container: every command goes through `provider.exec` against the
  * handle the request carries, so the mount table is the only filesystem the
  * model can reach. A driver constructed without a provider cannot be
  * constructed.
  *
  * This driver holds no credential (P12). The sandbox it runs in is provisioned
  * with a model relay — `MODEL_RELAY`, below — that holds the credential in a
  * container of its own and forwards the CLI's requests to the API with it. The
  * CLI is given the relay's address by the provider and a placeholder where a
  * key would go, so no process the task runs can read the credential: not the
  * CLI, not a tool it starts, not a later exec (D-P5-20, paid).
  *
  * Nor does it bound spend (P13). The relay meters every call with
  * `MODEL_METER` and enforces the task's budget where the task cannot reach it;
  * the CLI's own `--max-budget-usd` is not passed, so every stop is the
  * relay's and is in its log (D-P13-10).
  */
 import type {
   AgentClaim,
   CompiledRole,
   Driver,
   DriverCapabilities,
   DriverEvent,
   ModelFamily,
   ModelIdentity,
   ModelTier,
-  RoleId,
   TaskId,
   TaskRequest,
   TaskResult,
   Usage,
 } from '@olympus-ai/core';

   /**
    * The one in-flight invocation per sandbox: a second task on the same

    * that nothing enforced would be a capability claim with nothing behind it,
    * which is the state I8 exists to end.
    */
   readonly #inFlight = new Map<SandboxHandle, Promise<unknown>>();
   /**
    * The bound across sandboxes: invocations running now, and those waiting
    * for one to end, in arrival order. `capabilities().parallelism` is
    * `PARALLELISM`, and this is what makes it true (A-R4-04).
    */
   #running = 0;
   readonly #waiting: Array<() => void> = [];
   readonly #handlers = new Map<DriverEvent['kind'], Array<(e: DriverEvent) => void>>();
   /**

    * list, the MCP servers and the subagent types are facts the CLI printed

    */
   readonly #sessions = new Map<TaskId, SessionInit>();
   /**

    *

    * sandbox, and `#serialized` bounds concurrency per handle rather than across

    * field while a task on the first was still running — and that task's
    * artifacts would land in the other sandbox's mount table. The caller names
    * the sandbox it means; the driver does not infer one from whatever ran last.
    */
   #artifactSandbox: SandboxHandle | undefined;
 
   constructor(options: ClaudeCodeDriverOptions) {
     // eslint-disable-next-line @typescript-eslint/no-unnecessary-condition -- the type requires a provider, but this is the one class where a missing one means running the model on the host (I1). A caller arriving from untyped code is refused, not trusted.
     if (options.provider === undefined || options.provider === null) {
       refuse('provider', 'ClaudeCodeDriver needs the SandboxProvider that provisioned the handle; there is no host fallback (I1)');
     }
     this.#provider = options.provider;
     this.#workdir = options.workdir ?? '/workspace';
     this.#models = options.models ?? TIER_MODELS;
     this.#mcpServers = options.mcpServers ?? {};
     this.#settingsPath = requireSettingsOutsideWorkspace(options.settingsPath, this.#workdir);
   }
 
   provenanceId(): string {
     return `${this.id}@${CLI_VERSION}`;
   }
 
   /**
    * Every value is asserted against the real CLI by the conformance registry
    * (I8), in both directions: a `true` the CLI does not give is a false claim,
    * and a capability the CLI gives that is not declared here is an undeclared
    * one. Editing a value without changing what the driver does fails the suite.
    *
    * `computerUse` is `false` and stays false: the image has no display, and
    * the assertion proves the absence rather than skipping it. A container that
    * could drive one is R3.
    *

    * a message mid-turn over `--input-format stream-json`, but this driver does
    * not hold the session open to deliver one, and a capability is what the
    * driver does rather than what the tool could do.

   capabilities(): DriverCapabilities {
     return {
       subagents: true,
       hooks: true,
       mcp: true,
       // One CLI process per task, and a sandbox is one container: tasks run at
       // once only on separate sandboxes, at most this many (A-R4-04).

       computerUse: false,

     if (model === undefined) refuse('capability', `no model is mapped to tier '${tier}'`);
     return { provider: 'anthropic', family: FAMILY, model, version: CLI_VERSION };
   }

-    return this.#serialized(req.sandbox, () => this.#runTask(req, undefined));
+    return this.#serialized(req.sandbox, () => this.#runTask(req, req.role));

   /**

    *
    * `--agent` rather than hoping the model reaches for the `Task` tool: a
    * capability that depends on the model choosing to exercise it is not a
    * capability the driver can be held to. The role definition is passed on the
    * same argv, so a subagent is a session this driver configured rather than

    */
-  spawnSubagent(role: RoleId, req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> {
+  spawnSubagent(role: CompiledRole, req: TaskRequest): Promise<TaskResult> {
     return this.#serialized(req.sandbox, () => this.#runTask(req, role));
   }
 
   /**
    * One invocation per sandbox at a time, and no more than `PARALLELISM`
    * across sandboxes. A task waiting for its own sandbox holds no slot of the
    * bound, so it never keeps a task on another sandbox waiting.
    */
   async #serialized<T>(handle: SandboxHandle, body: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
     // Checked again after every wait: the call waited for may have been succeeded by another follower
     // that woke first, and the handle is claimed with no await between the check and the claim.
     for (let held = this.#inFlight.get(handle); held !== undefined; held = this.#inFlight.get(handle)) {
       await held.catch(() => undefined);
     }
     const running = this.#bounded(body);
     this.#inFlight.set(handle, running);
     try {
       return await running;
     } finally {
       if (this.#inFlight.get(handle) === running) this.#inFlight.delete(handle);
     }
   }
 
   /** Runs `body` once fewer than `PARALLELISM` invocations are running, in the order callers arrived. */
   async #bounded<T>(body: () => Promise<T>): Promise<T> {
     if (this.#running >= PARALLELISM) await new Promise<void>((admit) => this.#waiting.push(admit));
     else this.#running += 1;
     try {
       return await body();
     } finally {
       // A slot passes straight to the next caller waiting, so the count never dips to let a newcomer jump the queue.
       const next = this.#waiting.shift();
       if (next === undefined) this.#running -= 1;
       else next();
     }
   }

+    const invocation = this.#invocationFor(req, identity, role, configPath, ungranted);

     const stream = parseStream(exec.stdout);
     const init = this.#refuseUnusableStream(req, exec.exitCode, exec.stderr, stream);
+    // The role is what the session was configured with, read from what the CLI printed before it took a turn.

       claim: claimFrom(stream),

       // for, replaced by the model the session says it resolved to. `family`
       // is never touched by that replacement (I6).

   }
 
   /**
    * `steer` is deliberately absent, because `capabilities().steering` is
    * false. A method that threw would be a capability declared false and
    * present, which is the direction the conformance assertion refuses.
    */
 
   /**
    * Forgets a task's session. The container belongs to the run rather than to
    * this driver, so nothing here destroys it: a driver that tore down a
    * sandbox it did not provision would end every other task sharing it.
    */
   cancel(taskId: TaskId): Promise<void> {
     this.#sessions.delete(taskId);
     return Promise.resolve();
   }
 
   /**
    * Renders roles into the CLI's own artifact format under `targetDir`.
    *
    * Written through the sandbox like everything else, so the artifacts land on
    * the mount table rather than on the host. `CompiledRole.instructions` is
    * rendered verbatim: what the instructions should say is the compiler's
    * (M2), and this driver's job is the file format.
    */
   async emitArtifacts(roles: CompiledRole[], targetDir: string): Promise<void> {
     if (roles.length === 0) refuse('capability', 'emitArtifacts was given no roles; there is nothing to write');
     const handle = this.#artifactSandbox;
     if (handle === undefined) {
       refuse('provider', 'emitArtifacts has no sandbox to write into; call useSandbox() with the handle the run provisioned (I1)');
     }
     for (const file of artifactFiles(roles, targetDir)) {
       const written = await this.#provider.exec(handle, writeFileArgv(file.path), { env: { [ARTIFACT_CONTENT]: file.content } });
       if (written.exitCode !== 0) {
         refuse('invocation', `writing ${file.path} inside the sandbox failed with exit code ${String(written.exitCode)}: ${written.stderr.trim()}`);
       }
     }
   }
 
   on(kind: DriverEvent['kind'], handler: (e: DriverEvent) => void): void {
     const existing = this.#handlers.get(kind);
     if (existing === undefined) this.#handlers.set(kind, [handler]);
     else existing.push(handler);
   }
 
   /** Names the sandbox `emitArtifacts` writes into, for a caller emitting before any task has run. */
   useSandbox(handle: SandboxHandle): void {
     this.#artifactSandbox = handle;
   }
 
   /** What the CLI reported about a task's session, once one has run. */
   sessionFor(taskId: TaskId): SessionInit | undefined {
     return this.#sessions.get(taskId);
   }
 
   /**
    * I4: a granted tool this driver does not declare is refused before the task
    * starts. `validateToolGrants` applies this rule to a policy; this applies it
    * to the grant that actually reaches the CLI, so a caller that bypassed the
    * engine does not get a wider session than policy would have allowed.
    */
   #refuseUngrantedTools(req: TaskRequest): void {
     const declared = new Set(DECLARED_TOOLS);
     // MCP tools are not the driver's to declare — they belong to whichever
     // server a request configures — so they are checked against the server map
     // instead, by `#mcpConfigFor`.
     const undeclared = req.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) === undefined && !declared.has(tool));
     if (undeclared.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the task grants ${undeclared.map((t) => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}, which this driver does not declare. ` +
           'The task was not started: a grant this driver cannot honour is a policy that means something other than what it says (I4).',
       );
     }
   }
 
   /**
    * The MCP servers this request's grants name, and nothing else.
    *
    * A grant naming a server the driver does not hold is refused rather than
    * dropped: the CLI silently ignores a tool name it does not know, so a
    * policy could grant `mcp__missing__tool`, get a session without it, and
    * read as though the grant had taken effect (I4, I5).
    */
   #mcpConfigFor(req: TaskRequest): Record<string, McpServerConfig> {
     const wanted: Record<string, McpServerConfig> = {};
     for (const tool of req.tools) {
       const server = mcpServerOf(tool);
       if (server === undefined) continue;
       const config = this.#mcpServers[server];
       if (config === undefined) {
         refuse('grant', `the task grants '${tool}', but this driver holds no MCP server named '${server}'`);
       }
       wanted[server] = config;
     }
     return wanted;
   }
 
   /**
    * Every MCP tool the request's servers offer that the request did not grant.
    *
    * `--tools` governs the built-in set only: an MCP server contributes its
    * whole tool list to a session, so granting `mcp__s__a` on a server that also
    * offers `mcp__s__b` produces a session holding both. That is a tool
    * available to the model that policy did not grant, which is precisely what
    * I4 forbids, and it is not something a narrower grant can fix because the
    * names come from the server rather than from the driver.
    *
    * What does fix it is naming them: `--disallowedTools mcp__s__b` removes it
    * from the session entirely, not merely from what the model may use. So the
    * driver asks the CLI what the servers offer before it runs the task. The
    * inspection starts a session and reads the list it prints, which costs a
    * process and no tokens — the CLI prints its session before it calls
    * anything, and this invocation is given no tools and is bounded so it
    * cannot sit there retrying.
    *
    * Only when the request grants an MCP tool at all. A task with no MCP grant
    * configures no server, so there is nothing to inspect and nothing to hide.
    */
   async #ungrantedMcpTools(req: TaskRequest, servers: Record<string, McpServerConfig>, configPath: string | undefined): Promise<string[]> {
     if (configPath === undefined || Object.keys(servers).length === 0) return [];
     const inspected = await this.#provider.exec(
       req.sandbox,
       [
         'sh',
         '-c',
         `mkdir -p "$HOME" && cd ${shellQuote(this.#workdir)} && exec timeout ${String(INSPECT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS)} "$@"`,
         'driver',
         'claude',
         '--print', 'the session is read before any turn',
         '--output-format', 'stream-json',
         '--verbose',
         '--tools', '',
         '--setting-sources', '',
         '--strict-mcp-config',
         '--mcp-config', configPath,
         '--permission-mode', 'bypassPermissions',
         '--permission-prompts', 'none',
         '--no-session-persistence',
       ],
       { env: CLI_KEY_ENVIRONMENT },
     );
     const init = parseStream(inspected.stdout).init;
     if (init === undefined) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the MCP servers ${Object.keys(servers).join(', ')} could not be inspected, so the tools they offer beyond the grant are unknown. ` +
           'The task was not started: running it would mean offering the model tools nobody can enumerate (I4, I5).',
       );
     }
     // An `init` line is not an enumeration. A server that came up failed, or
     // was still connecting, contributes no tools to the list, so a disallow
     // list computed from it would be short by exactly the tools that server
     // offers -- and the task would then run holding them. Every configured
     // server has to be connected, and every granted tool has to be present,
     // before the absence of a name means anything (I5).
     const unconnected = init.mcpServers.filter((server) => server.status !== 'connected');
     if (unconnected.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `MCP server(s) ${unconnected.map((server) => `${server.name} (${server.status})`).join(', ')} did not connect during inspection, ` +
           'so what they offer is unknown and the tools to disallow cannot be computed',
       );
     }
     const enumerated = new Set(init.tools);
     const absent = req.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) !== undefined && !enumerated.has(tool));
     if (absent.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the inspection did not find granted MCP tool(s) ${absent.join(', ')}; the servers offer a different set than the request names`,
       );
     }
     const granted = new Set(req.tools);
     return init.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) !== undefined && !granted.has(tool));
   }
 
   /**
    * Writes the MCP configuration into the container and returns its path, or
    * `undefined` when the request granted no MCP tool.
    *
    * A file rather than a JSON string on the argv. The CLI takes `--mcp-config`
    * as "JSON files or strings (space-separated)", and a server definition that
    * carries a command with arguments has spaces in it, so passing one inline
    * produced a session with no MCP tools at all — and the model, asked to use
    * one, invented a tool list rather than saying it had none. A path has no
    * spaces and is what the documented file form expects.
    *
    * It lives in the container's own filesystem rather than the workspace mount,
    * which keeps it out of the tree a task works on and out of any diff. That is
    * all the location buys. The driver and the agent's own commands run as the
    * same user, so a process the agent started can rewrite this file between the
    * inspection and the run. What makes that pointless is not where the file
    * sits but `#refuseSessionUnlikeGrant`, which compares the session the CLI
    * actually started against the grant and refuses a tool nobody granted
    * however it arrived.
    *
    * Nothing secret is in it, and `McpServerConfig` has no field that could
    * carry one: a server environment was removed from the type rather than left
    * as a place a caller might put a token the agent could then read.
    */
   async #writeMcpConfig(req: TaskRequest, servers: Record<string, McpServerConfig>): Promise<string | undefined> {
     if (Object.keys(servers).length === 0) return undefined;
     const path = `/tmp/mcp-${sessionIdFor(req.taskId)}.json`;
     const written = await this.#provider.exec(req.sandbox, writeFileArgv(path), {
       env: { [ARTIFACT_CONTENT]: JSON.stringify({ mcpServers: servers }) },
     });
     if (written.exitCode !== 0) {
       refuse('invocation', `the MCP configuration could not be written to ${path}: ${written.stderr.trim()}`);
     }
     return path;
   }
 
   #invocationFor(
     req: TaskRequest,
     identity: ModelIdentity,
-    asRole: RoleId | undefined,
+    role: CompiledRole,
     configPath: string | undefined,
     ungrantedMcpTools: readonly string[],
   ): Invocation {
     // `--tools` and not `--allowedTools`: the first decides which tools exist
     // in the session at all, the second decides which uses need approval. I4
     // is about availability — a tool outside the grant must be unavailable to
     // the model, not merely unused — so the narrower flag is the correct one.
     // An empty grant produces `--tools ''`, which is a session with no tools:
     // default deny, spelled the way the CLI spells it.
     const cli = [
       'claude',
       '--print', req.variableSuffix,
       '--output-format', 'stream-json',
       '--verbose',
       '--model', identity.model,
       // The cacheable half. Appended rather than replacing the default prompt,
       // so the session still behaves like the CLI it is, and the dynamic
       // sections that change between tasks — cwd, git status, env — are moved
       // out of the prefix, so two tasks in one run present the same prefix and
       // the second reads it from cache instead of writing it again.
       '--append-system-prompt', req.stablePrefix,
       '--exclude-dynamic-system-prompt-sections',
       '--tools', req.tools.filter((tool) => mcpServerOf(tool) === undefined).join(','),
       // Nothing outside this argv configures the session: no user, project, or
       // local settings file, and no MCP server the request did not name. A
       // session configured by a settings file in the workspace would be
       // configured by the thing it is being judged on (I3).
       '--setting-sources', '',
       '--strict-mcp-config',
       // The container is the sandbox. A permission prompt has nobody to answer
       // it, and `none` denies anything that would prompt rather than stalling
       // until the wall-clock budget kills the container.
       '--permission-mode', 'bypassPermissions',
       '--permission-prompts', 'none',
       '--no-session-persistence',
       '--session-id', sessionIdFor(req.taskId),
       '--include-hook-events',
     ];
     if (configPath !== undefined) cli.push('--mcp-config', configPath);
     // Named one by one, because a server-wide pattern removes the granted tools
     // with the rest and a later `--allowedTools` does not bring them back.
     if (ungrantedMcpTools.length > 0) cli.push('--disallowedTools', ungrantedMcpTools.join(','));
     if (this.#settingsPath !== undefined) cli.push('--settings', this.#settingsPath);
-    if (asRole !== undefined) {
-      cli.push('--agents', JSON.stringify({ [asRole]: { description: `the ${asRole} role`, prompt: req.stablePrefix } }), '--agent', asRole);
-    }
+    // The role, as the agent definition the session runs as: its instructions and nothing else, so no
+    // field in it can widen the tools or change the model above (I4). It sits in the system prompt
+    // with the granted context appended after it, so two tasks under one role share a prefix (D-A-RC-03).
+    cli.push('--agents', JSON.stringify({ [role.role]: { description: `the ${role.role} role`, prompt: role.instructions } }), '--agent', role.role);
     // `exec "$@"` runs the CLI as the process itself, with every argument an
     // argv element the shell never re-reads. Only the working directory is
     // interpolated, and it is quoted.
     return {
       argv: ['sh', '-c', `mkdir -p "$HOME" && cd ${shellQuote(this.#workdir)} && exec "$@"`, 'driver', ...cli],
       env: environmentFor(identity.model),
     };
   }
 
   /**
    * Fail closed on anything that means the task did not run (I5), and return
    * the session that did.
    *
    * A session the CLI never started, a credential from a source this driver
    * did not arrange, a missing result, and an API status the CLI gave up on
    * are each a refusal. None of them is a `TaskResult` with a sad narrative in
    * it: a `TaskResult` says a task ran, and the runtime derives status from
    * evidence that assumes one did.
    */
   #refuseUnusableStream(req: TaskRequest, exitCode: number, stderr: string, stream: ParsedStream): NonNullable<ParsedStream['init']> {
     const init = stream.init;
     if (init === undefined) {
       const first = stream.unreadable[0];
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the CLI produced no session for task ${req.taskId} (exit code ${String(exitCode)})` +
           (first === undefined ? '' : `; the first unreadable line was ${JSON.stringify(first.slice(0, 200))}`) +
           (stderr.trim() === '' ? '' : `; stderr: ${stderr.trim().slice(0, 400)}`),
       );
     }
     if (init.apiKeySource !== KEY_VARIABLE) {
       refuse(
         'credential',
         `the session authenticated from '${init.apiKeySource}' rather than ${KEY_VARIABLE}; ` +
           'a key this driver did not supply is one it cannot account for, and one the CLI found is a credential inside the sandbox',
       );
     }
     const result = stream.result;
     if (result === undefined) {
       refuse('invocation', `the CLI ended without a result for task ${req.taskId} (exit code ${String(exitCode)})`);
     }
     if (result.apiErrorStatus !== undefined) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the model was not reached for task ${req.taskId}: the API answered ${String(result.apiErrorStatus)} and the CLI stopped retrying` +
           (result.narrative.trim() === '' ? '' : `; the CLI reported ${JSON.stringify(result.narrative.trim().slice(0, 400))}`),
       );
     }
     if (result.isError && result.numTurns === 0) {
       refuse('invocation', `the CLI took no turn for task ${req.taskId}: ${result.terminalReason ?? 'no reason given'}`);
     }
     // Nothing was refused mid-task. Under `bypassPermissions` there is nothing
     // to prompt and therefore nothing to deny, so a denial means the task ran
     // with less than it was granted, and the result describes work done under
     // conditions the request did not ask for (I5).
     if (result.permissionDenials.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `task ${req.taskId} had ${String(result.permissionDenials.length)} tool use(s) denied ` +
           `(${result.permissionDenials.join(', ')}), so it did not run under the capabilities it was granted`,
       );
     }
     this.#refuseSessionUnlikeGrant(req, init);
     return init;
   }
 
   /**
    * The session the CLI actually started must offer exactly what the request
    * granted: no more, and no less.
    *
    * Everything else in this driver is an argument it hopes the CLI honours --
    * `--tools`, `--disallowedTools`, `--mcp-config`, `--strict-mcp-config`. This
    * is the one place that checks. The CLI prints what the session came up with
    * before it takes a turn, so the check costs nothing, and it is the backstop
    * for every assumption those flags rest on. Two of those assumptions were
    * already wrong before anyone read the session report: an inline MCP
    * configuration silently produced no servers at all, and a granted server
    * contributed tools nobody granted.
    *
    * Both directions refuse. A tool the session holds and the request did not
    * grant is a capability arriving from somewhere other than policy, which is
    * the whole of I4. A tool the request granted and the session does not hold
    * is a grant that did not take effect, and a task that runs anyway produces a
    * result describing work done under conditions nobody asked for, which is the
    * silent degrade I5 refuses.
    */
   #refuseSessionUnlikeGrant(req: TaskRequest, init: SessionInit): void {
     const granted = new Set(req.tools);
     const offered = new Set(init.tools);
     const ungranted = [...offered].filter((tool) => !granted.has(tool)).sort((a, b) => a.localeCompare(b));
     const missing = [...granted].filter((tool) => !offered.has(tool)).sort((a, b) => a.localeCompare(b));
     if (ungranted.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} offers ${ungranted.map((t) => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}, which the request did not grant. ` +
           'A capability the model can reach and policy did not grant is the failure I4 exists to prevent.',
       );
     }
     if (missing.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} does not offer ${missing.map((t) => `'${t}'`).join(', ')}, which the request granted. ` +
           'The grant did not take effect, and a task run without it would describe work done under conditions nobody asked for (I5).',
       );
     }
     const configured = new Set(Object.keys(this.#mcpServersFor(req)));
     const loaded = init.mcpServers.map((server) => server.name);
     const unexpected = loaded.filter((name) => !configured.has(name));
     if (unexpected.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'grant',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} loaded MCP server(s) ${unexpected.join(', ')}, which this driver did not configure`,
       );
     }
     const broken = init.mcpServers.filter((server) => server.status !== 'connected');
     if (broken.length > 0) {
       refuse(
         'invocation',
         `the session for task ${req.taskId} has MCP server(s) ${broken.map((b) => `${b.name} (${b.status})`).join(', ')} that are not connected; ` +
           'the granted tools were not reachable',
       );
     }
   }
 
   /** The servers this request's grants name. `#mcpConfigFor` refuses an unheld one; this one is for a grant already validated. */
   #mcpServersFor(req: TaskRequest): Record<string, McpServerConfig> {
     const wanted: Record<string, McpServerConfig> = {};
     for (const tool of req.tools) {
       const server = mcpServerOf(tool);
       if (server === undefined) continue;
       const config = this.#mcpServers[server];
       if (config !== undefined) wanted[server] = config;
     }
     return wanted;
   }
 
   #emit(event: DriverEvent): void {
     for (const handler of this.#handlers.get(event.kind) ?? []) handler(event);
   }
 }
 
 /**
  * The event stream, built from what the CLI reported rather than from what the
  * model said about itself.
  *
  * These are observations of the invocation, and the driver sees only what the
  * CLI emits: a write the CLI did not report is a write this list does not
  * have. That is exactly why P6 collects the diff itself and why `filesChanged`
  * lives on `AgentClaim` (D-P4-01).
  */

     const wrote = provider.calls.find((call) => call.env?.[ARTIFACT_CONTENT]?.includes('FACTORY_ARTIFACT_EOF') === true);
     expect(wrote).toBeDefined();
     expect(wrote?.cmd).toEqual(writeFileArgv('/t/agents/r.md'));
   });
 });

 /**
  * Provisions a sandbox, builds the image if the host does not hold it, and
  * hands `body` a driver wired to both. Everything is torn down when `body`
  * settles, whether it threw or not.
  */
+/** A role whose instructions a session that loaded it can be seen to follow, and one that did not cannot. */
+export const PROBE_ROLE: CompiledRole = {
+  role: 'builder' as RoleId,
+  instructions: 'You are the builder role of a test harness. Do exactly what the message asks and nothing more.',
+  sha256: '0'.repeat(64),
+};
+
 export async function withDriver<T>(
   body: (h: Harness) => Promise<T>,
   options: Partial<Omit<ClaudeCodeDriverOptions, 'provider'>> = {},
 ): Promise<T> {
   const secret = credential();
   // One nonce per harness, so every task of this run shares a prefix that no
   // earlier run has ever presented to the cache.
   const prefix = stablePrefixFor(randomUUID());
   const image = await ensureImage();
   const base = await mkdtemp(join(tmpdir(), 'factory-driver-'));
   const workspaceDir = join(base, 'workspace');
   const vaultDir = join(base, 'vault');
   await mkdir(workspaceDir, { recursive: true });
   await mkdir(vaultDir, { recursive: true });
   // The container runs as a fixed user, and a bind mount carries the host's
   // ownership straight through: a workspace owned by any other uid is not
   // writable by the task, and the one thing the task is supposed to be able to
   // write is its workspace. On a developer's machine the two usually coincide
   // and this is a no-op; on a GitHub runner they do not -- the runner is uid
   // 1001 and the image's user is 1000 -- and without this the I1 assertion
   // fails having proved nothing about where commands ran.
   //
   // This makes the *fixture* usable. It does not fix the underlying constraint,
   // which is recorded as a known limit: a runtime that creates a workspace as a
   // uid the image does not run as hands the agent a read-only workspace.
   await chmod(workspaceDir, 0o777);
   const { LocalDockerProvider: Provider } = await import('@olympus-ai/sandbox');
   // A Vault root that exists and holds nothing: the provider requires one, and
   // no mount here may be it, sit inside it, or contain it (I1).
   // The credential goes to the provider, which gives it to the relay and to nothing else.
   const provider = await Provider.create({ vaultPaths: [vaultDir], credentials: { [MODEL_CREDENTIAL]: secret } });
   const handle = await provider.provision(spec(image, workspaceDir));
 
   // The driver is given a provider that reports each exec as it starts and
   // ends. It is the real provider underneath — nothing is stubbed — so an
   // assertion about concurrency reads the same containers everything else does.
   let watcher: ((sandbox: SandboxHandle) => () => void) | undefined;
   const extra: SandboxHandle[] = [];
   // The CLI's own account, per sandbox: each sandbox's relay meters only the sessions run in it.
   const counts = new Map<SandboxHandle, TokenCounts>();
   const countsFor = (h: SandboxHandle): TokenCounts => {
     let c = counts.get(h);
     if (c === undefined) {
       c = { inputTokens: 0, outputTokens: 0, cacheReadTokens: 0, cacheWriteTokens: 0 };
       counts.set(h, c);
     }
     return c;
   };
   const test = expect.getState().currentTestName ?? '(outside a test)';
   const watched: SandboxProvider = {
     id: provider.id,
     provision: (s: SandboxSpec) => provider.provision(s),
     destroy: (h: SandboxHandle) => provider.destroy(h),
     capabilities: () => provider.capabilities(),
     exec: async (h: SandboxHandle, cmd: string[], o?: ExecOptions) => {
       const leave = watcher?.(h);
       try {
         const result = await provider.exec(h, cmd, o);
         recordCost(result.stdout);
         sessionUsage(result.stdout, countsFor(h));
         return result;
       } finally {
         leave?.();
       }
     },
   };
   const driver = new ClaudeCodeDriver({ provider: watched, workdir: WORKDIR, ...options });
   // The run tells the driver which sandbox its artifacts belong in. `emitArtifacts`
   // takes a target directory and no handle, so a driver that has not been told
   // refuses rather than writing to the host (I1) — which is what it did the first
   // time this harness ran, because nothing here had said.
   driver.useSandbox(handle);
 
   const harness: Harness = {
     stablePrefix: prefix,
     driver,
     provider,
     handle,
     workspaceDir,
     request: (overrides: Partial<TaskRequest> = {}): TaskRequest => ({
       taskId: 'probe' as TaskId,
-      role: 'builder' as TaskRequest['role'],
+      role: PROBE_ROLE,
       stablePrefix: prefix,
       variableSuffix: 'Reply with the single word: ready',
       tier: 'fast',
       tools: [],
       sandbox: handle,
       timeoutMs: 300_000,
       budget: BUDGET,
       ...overrides,
     }),
     observeExec: (enter: (sandbox: SandboxHandle) => () => void): void => {
       watcher = enter;
     },
     provisionMore: async (count: number): Promise<SandboxHandle[]> => {
       for (let i = 0; i < count; i += 1) {
         const dir = join(base, `workspace-${String(extra.length)}`);
         await mkdir(dir, { recursive: true });
         await chmod(dir, 0o777);
         extra.push(await provider.provision(spec(image, dir)));
       }
       return extra.slice(-count);
     },
   };
 
   try {
     return await body(harness);
   } finally {
     // The reading is kept, or why it could not be taken: the meter control fails on either.
     const reading = await provider.destroy(handle).then((t) => t.meter).catch((error: unknown) => (error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error))));
     meteredSessions.push({ test, reading, cli: countsFor(handle) });
     // Each extra sandbox's relay metered its own calls, and each reading is kept beside the first's.
     for (const more of extra) {
       const read = await provider.destroy(more).then((t) => t.meter).catch((error: unknown) => (error instanceof Error ? error : new Error(String(error))));
       meteredSessions.push({ test, reading: read, cli: countsFor(more) });
     }
     await rm(base, { recursive: true, force: true });
   }
 }
 
 /**
  * The invariant half of the context: identical across every task of one run,
  * and different from every other run's.
  *
  * The nonce is what makes the cache measurement mean anything. The CLI's own
  * system prompt is large, identical between sessions, and cached account-wide,
  * so by the time this assertion runs, earlier tasks in the same suite have
  * already warmed it and a first task reads thousands of tokens from cache
  * before it has done anything. A prefix no session has ever presented cannot
  * be read from cache, so the first task must write it and only the second can
  * read it — which is the thing the split is supposed to buy.
  */
